## Summary

Boarding & Salvage is the sortie: the rules that compose Interaction, Suit & Equipment, Modular Ship Assembly, Derelict Authoring and the Item & Module Database into the game's five-minute loop of entering a dark derelict, reading it by one's own light, cutting one module free, carrying it home slower than one came under a rising Attention, and seating it in the home ship. It registers no state, hook, command, mutator, read view or registry constant; it owns the order and cost of the steps, the carry-admitted set, the pod-hop cargo rule, the field-check procedure, the retreat and abandon rules, the stub-capping policy and the content-side sortie constraints. The player feels it as a held breath: read the wreck, spend light, noise and air on one take that cannot be put back, and carry it home with the dark leaning in. It serves P3 and P5 (primary), P1 (as a constraint), and P4 and P2.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Interaction System (Designed), Suit & Equipment (Designed), Modular Ship Assembly (Designed), Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) (Designed), Item & Module Database (Designed)`; indirect: Player Controller, Life-Support Graph, Attention, Web Map & Transit (Designed); Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table and Debug Overlay (all Designed as of 2026-10-27; counterpart rows PF:550, AA:262, HS-43, IW:505, CT-33) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; the contracts with systems still undesigned (Onboarding, Procedural Derelict Generation, Depth Rings, Knowledge Codex, Failure Contract, Co-op) are provisional

## Overview

Boarding & Salvage is the **sortie**: the rules that compose the game's other systems into the five-minute loop of the concept (Core Loop: fly the pod to a derelict, board it, find one salvageable system, extract it under rising Attention, return, weld or install it). It is a **composition system with zero runtime footprint**: it registers no state block, no step hook, no command type, no mutator, no read view, no notification and no registry constant (BS-1). Every verb in the index wording already has a designed owner (the pod hop, the lit act gate, the cut and cap channels, `pickup` and `drop`, the carried instance, the install, the Attention push); this GDD owns only what none of them owns: the **order and cost of the steps** as a design (the sortie sequence), the **carry-admitted set** that lets the haul reach home, the **pod-hop cargo rule**, the **field-check procedure** for suspect salvage, the **retreat and abandon rules** that make restraint a strategy, the **stub-capping policy**, the **severed-stub art direction**, the content-side sortie constraints it cites from Derelict Authoring, a set of design-time formulas, the Visual/Audio and Game Feel direction of the boarding rhythm, and the acceptance that proves a scripted sortie runs end to end. It is the one system where the player *spends*: light, noise, air, time and a take that cannot be put back.

**How the player meets it.** They never meet a "salvage screen"; they meet a dark dock and a held breath. They ride the pod to a wreck, arrive in their own chest-lamp beam only, and **stand still** (listening is a stance, not a verb: an idle actor pushes no noise, and the Graph's hiss, creak and whistle, Attention's floor-gated hull-settling creaks (AT-26) and, once a tell opens, its structure-borne tell bed (AT-15, AUD-4) are all there is to hear). They light the room they intend to work in, **read the world** (a dried frost fan at a lagged elbow, a dead lantern, dust in the cone, the stub a given module would leave) and **decide** which one thing is worth its noise, with no value tag, rating or condition bar anywhere in the game. They cut the module free with a four-second lit channel that ends in a loud report the web hears, optionally cap the severed stubs that hiss from their own cut, lift the yield with hands that are now full, and **carry it home** at a third of a run's speed, every other prompt in the world gone but `G . Drop` until the pod console or a fitting hardpoint offers its one press. A tell may open on the way: they keep walking, or set the module down and run, and either is legal. They press the pod console with the module still in their arms, ride the hush home, and seat it in the vacant hardpoint, where it works and, because they put it there, costs them something new. The player finishes a sortie able to point at a module at home and say where it came from and what it cost.

**What the game loses without it.** The concept's MVP hypothesis (the boarding-to-breach two-rhythm loop is tense and absorbing for a 30+ minute solo session) would have a breach rhythm and a life-support graph but no sortie to be tense *about*; the weld and the install would have nothing to install; the field-check ladder of Pillar 4 would have no moment at which it is run; and "retreat and restraint are real strategies" would be a slogan with no declared conditions. Without it there is no sortie.

**Index wording reconciled.** The index (Systems Enumeration 15; Recommended Design Order 20) reads: "The boarding rhythm: enter, listen, light, decide, cut a module free, carry it home under rising Attention; field checks for suspect salvage." This GDD adopts that wording as the **shape** of the sequence and adjusts three words. (1) *Rhythm* is not a Boarding mechanic: the boarding, tell and breach rhythms are the art bible's (ART 2.6 to 2.8) and Attention's, and Boarding contributes to them only through costs the player chooses to pay (see Game Feel). (2) *Cut a module free* and *carry it home* are Interaction's verbs and the Controller's gait; Boarding states how the loop **uses** them, never how they work. (3) *Spawn* is not used for derelict A (Derelict Authoring DA-2 forbids it): A is *boarded*, *reached* or *found*. The Life-Support Graph's LS-26 and the index still say "spawn" for A; the wording refresh is routed in Cross-References, not edited.

| At a glance (MVP) | Value | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| New state blocks / hooks / commands / mutators / read views / registry constants | **0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0** | BS-1 (rulings R1, R27) |
| Sortie steps composed | 14 (prepare, board, enter, listen, light, decide, position, cut, cap-or-leave, pick up, carry, hop, install, settle) | BS-2 |
| Verbs the loop uses (all owned elsewhere) | `begin_cut`, `begin_cap`, `pickup`, `drop`, `field_check`, `throw_glowstick`, `toggle_beam`, `equip_tool`, `pod_hop`, `install_module` | Interaction, Suit, Web Map, Assembly |
| Carry-admitted interactions | exactly 3: `drop`, `install_module` on a compatible HARDPOINT, `pod_hop` on the pod CONSOLE | BS-9 |
| Modules that ride one hop per seat | 1 (`carry_ref`) plus the STOWED stacks | BS-12 |
| Reference payoff of the first sortie | `htr_a` to `hp_cargo_heater` (the cut crosses the one lethal-cold room) | BS-17 |
| Class (d) phantom salvage | **not in MVP**; hooks defined, acceptance deferred | BS-14 |
| Combat, progression, economy, readouts | none, by rule | BS-24 |

**MVP slice.** Ships: the 14-step sortie on home, derelict A and derelict B with one seat; the reference cut `htr_a` to `hp_cargo_heater`; `scr_a` as optional restraint; the carry-admitted set (BS-9; ASK-BS-I1 answered); rungs 1 (perception only), 2 (SCAN), 4 and 5 of the field-check ladder; class (b) lies only; overhead carry art and the open-end/cap/kerf pieces. Later: class (d) phantom salvage, HEFT and PALETTE checks (VS-A); cargo cradle option B (VS); co-op seats, revive and crewmate-as-horror (VS-B); the yield settle animation (VS).

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What the MVP loop pays once.** As specced, the MVP install payoff happens exactly once: the reference sortie to `htr_a`, installed in the one vacant hardpoint that fits (`hp_cargo_heater`, MSA:28), is the only taught install, so the "decide" step is scripted (there is one fit) and every sortie after the first pays nothing. `scr_a` has no destination, so a correct read of it changes nothing: a healthy scrubber and a dead one have the same outcome. The design does not hide this. The content route that would give the loop a second and third payoff (a vacant scrubber hardpoint at home, ideally with two or three competing hardpoints, routed to Modular Ship Assembly and Derelict Authoring) is a scope decision and is **OQ-BS-3 (USER DECISION)**, recommended now and not after a failed playtest. Until it is ruled, the `scr_a` beat is read as flavour (the honest "time the loot was garbage"), and the tests that need a second payoff (X-BS-9, the strip-the-wreck degenerate-strategy cases) are conditional on it.

## Player Fantasy

**"I came here to take one thing, and now the ship knows I took it."**

Boarding & Salvage is the game's *commitment* system. Every other system lets the player observe, read, conserve and repair; this one asks them to **spend** (light, noise, air, time) and to **take** something that cannot be put back. The fantasy is not loot and not power. It is the held-breath competence of a salvage diver on bottom time: read the wreck, choose the one piece worth its cost, cut it free, and carry it home slower than you came, with the dark leaning in. It serves the core fantasy directly (a body assembled from the corpses of other ships): the sortie is how a corpse's organ becomes part of the living ship, and the player should be able to say, of any module at home, where it came from and what it cost.

*(Framing choice, logged: three candidates were weighed. **A, "Take One Thing Home"** — direct, matches the concept loop verbatim — is the frame. **B, "Read the Corpse"** (forensic archaeology) is kept as the *second beat inside A*, because the reading is already Derelict Authoring's authored beat and the art bible's verdict grammar and under-sells the commitment as a frame. **C, "The Organ Harvest"** is rejected as a frame and kept as design-prose vocabulary only — graft, severed, the stub bleeds gas — because a body metaphor that reached a sprite, a palette or a sound brief would spend the flesh and violet signal that Pillar 1 reserves for sanity leaks; see the guard rail in BS-22 and BS-24.)*

### Pillars served (verbatim text, concept Game Pillars)

| Pillar | Text served | What the fantasy does with it |
|---|---|---|
| **P3 The Web Watches** (primary) | "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention ... Restraint and stealth are real strategies." | The cut is the loudest thing the player chooses to do aboard (`SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 [PLACEHOLDER] at `U_TOOL` 4 plus one `U_LOUD` 180, both Attention's [PLACEHOLDER] units); the walk home is the slowest. Not cutting, leaving it, and setting it down are full strategies, not failures. |
| **P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total** (primary) | "The outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear (scrubbers degrade, salvage runs out), so there is no safe place to farm forever." | What you take is gone from the wreck forever (salvage-once, IDB-17; the authored node list is the faucet, DA-21). The haul *is* the ship's future body. |
| **P1 The World Never Lies** (primary constraint) | "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth." | The cut leaves a kerf and a hissing stub; the carry is felt as breath, never read as a number; a module's worth is read from the world. |
| **P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality** (serves) | "(d) Phantom salvage. A crate of treasure is garbage once it is home. At least one cheaper field check exists (carried weight, a second scanner, the palette leak); the trip home is the fallback, not the only reveal." | The prize may lie; the price never does (below). |
| **P2 Dread Before Chaos** (serves) | "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived." | A carry is the moment a tell hurts most; the drop-and-run is always legal, so the tell is a decision and not a sentence. |

### Anchor moment: "The Weight of the Kerf"

The single moment this GDD must make a reader feel, built only from locked facts and set on the **reference sortie** (the first sortie to derelict A, taught payoff `htr_a` to `hp_cargo_heater`, BS-17), never on the optional `scr_a`.

1. **The approach.** From the pod, derelict A is black but for one Tungsten point: somebody else's lantern (DA-27, ART 2.5). The player arrives at `a_dock` in their own beam only (ART 2.6: "The beam is the only warmth in frame").
2. **The read.** They stand still. Standing still *is* the listening. Dried coolant frost at a lagged elbow, a dead lantern on a machine, dust in the cone (DA beat 2, ART 6.5). The wreck has told them what killed it; it has not told them what to take.
3. **The decision.** The heater. Medium, power-only, so it will leave no stub and no hiss (IDB-7); home has exactly one hardpoint it fits (MSA:28). This is "weigh a module's value against the noise of taking it" (concept Key Dynamics): the value lives in the player's head, in what home lacks, never on the module (BS-6).
4. **The crossing.** The heater is in the one room that will kill them if they linger: lethal cold (DA-28). They have seen the frost creep over the sill of the closed door before opening it (P2: the tell precedes the hazard). The crossing is bounded by the authored hazard rules (at most 7 tiles of lethal cold, DA-28); the dwell is a thing they *measure with their own breath*, not a bar.
5. **The cut.** The cutter bites. Four seconds of channel (`SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 [PLACEHOLDER]) on a linear bar (ART 7.3, never a ring), TOOL noise every accrued tick, and at completion one LOUD report the player *knows* went out into the web. The kerf scorch stays on the pedestal (ART 6.5: "a module cut out cleanly by someone with the right tool"). The yield lies on the deck at the node anchor; it is not in their hands yet (IA-25). **This is the point of no return**: the noise is spent whether or not they now carry it.
6. **The lift.** A lit TAP. Hands full. The breath changes under the load (SE-28). The gait drops to `V_CARRY` 1 px/tick, a third of a run, and nothing will make it faster (carry is not scaled by mass). Every other prompt in the world disappears except `G . Drop` (IA-5), until the pod console or a fitting hardpoint offers its one press (BS-9): the screen itself says *you have one job now*.
7. **The walk home.** 48 ticks and 48 AU per tile (IDB F-13; the AU rest on `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry 1 [PLACEHOLDER], a placeholder unit). At the cone's edge the filaments begin to resolve (ART 2.7: "It noticed."). The player now owns the most interesting decision in the game: *keep walking, or put it down and run.*
8. **The hush.** The console press with the module still in their arms (BS-9, BS-12; PCI:694 "the module travels with the pod"). Pod-transit silence (ART 2.5, "Smallness").
9. **The graft.** At home, the install TAP (MSA-26); the cargo bay heater comes alive and, because the player put it there, **arms the home ship's power-overload risk** (DA:855). The prize has a cost even after it is home. That is the game.

| Beat | Time (design-time, lower bound) | Raw Attention push (before leakage; excludes light and power terms) | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cut `htr_a` | `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 [PLACEHOLDER] ticks = 4.0 s | 240 x 4 + 180 = **1140 AU**, 19 % of `ATT_THRESHOLD` 6000 | `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD` (Attention, [PLACEHOLDER]) |
| Cap | none: the heater leaves no stub | 0 | IDB-7 |
| Carry, D tiles | D x 48 ticks = D x 0.8 s | D x 48 | IDB F-13 (`NOISE_PER_TICK` carry 1 [PLACEHOLDER]) |
| Pod leg | Web Map's `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] (hands free) | 0 | WM-34 |

The numbers make one point: **the cut costs about four seconds and the carry costs about a tile per second of exposure**; the cut is the commitment, the carry is the consequence. Neither may become trivial. If playtest finds the carry the boring part, the lever is route length through Derelict Authoring's bounds and Attention's placeholders, never `V_CARRY` (locked) and never a pressure the world does not show.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Steps 6 and 7 are a design target that the current numbers do not deliver, and a retune of one constant will not deliver them.** Four findings, each recomputed from D.2 and D.3 (the figures are the review's recomputation, `[UNV: Attention to confirm]`): (1) *No crossing.* The lit reference peak is 57 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD` and the corner of the safe ranges reaches only 65 percent. Attention deliberately keeps walking with the beam below `theta_HOLD` (a steady state of about 3,997 AU), so a longer route never crosses; only the intake rate `r` does, and it would need about 3.4 times today's carry intake. (2) *No live choice.* If a tell did open from the sortie's own noise, the `htr_a` carry (624 ticks) fits every played tell window with 36 to 276 ticks to spare (D.3), so walking on always wins and "keep walking, or set it down" is not yet a choice. (3) *Not caused by the take.* About 28 percent of the threshold is home-ship idle before the player boards (`L0` 1,678.5 AU, D.2), so lifting `W_L`, `W_P` or `B_table` to open a tell would fire tells the player cannot blame on their cut. (4) *A free third option.* After the cut, standing still lets the tell lapse for about 1 percent of sanity, so freezing is a free third option (D.3, EC-BS-27). This is the P3 core promise and the MVP kill/continue hypothesis, so it is escalated as a ruling across Boarding, Attention and Derelict Authoring (**OQ-BS-27, USER DECISION**) and not repaired here. Until it is ruled, steps 6 and 7 are the intended anchor and not a delivered one, and no story opens for BS-13 or the tell acceptance rows.

### The design test

> *If we are debating between making the haul easier to bring home and making the decision to take it harder to avoid, we choose the decision.*

| Debate | The test picks | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| Auto-carry a heavy yield vs a lit pickup TAP | the TAP: the player chooses to lift, in light | BS-9 |
| A pod cradle that hauls three modules vs one carried per seat | one per seat at MVP: each module is its own trip, fuel and walk | BS-12 |
| A value tag that ranks modules vs reading what home lacks | reading: the decision stays the player's | BS-6 |
| Forcing a drop on a tell vs leaving it to the player | the player's drop | BS-13 |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the row "mandatory capping vs optional priced capping" is removed. At the MVP capping is never the better option on a derelict you abandon (leaving costs 0.4 percent of the room's gas, D.5, while capping costs 90 ticks and 360 AU, about 6 percent of the threshold), so presenting cap-or-leave as a tested choice overstated it. It stays an optional, priced act in BS-8 and returns to the design test only if a world-visible P3 stake is ruled for the stub; none is invented here.)*

Pillar-level tests quoted alongside: **P3** an extraction that can be done dark and free vs one that must be lit and noisy, choose lit and noisy; **P5** a refillable crate vs a finite node list that stays emptied, choose finite; **P1** a value tag vs the stub hiss and the load breath, choose hiss and breath; **P4** a check that costs nothing vs one under 5 % of the trip home (Sanity's `C_check <= 0.05 x T_home`), choose the cheap-but-not-free one; **P2** a carried module that cannot be abandoned vs one that can be dropped, choose droppable.

### How the fantasy deepens under hallucination

**The lie is in the prize, never in the price (BS-15).** A hallucination may lie about *what* a module is or *whether* it is worth taking. It may never lie about *what taking it costs*: the cut's noise, the stub's hiss, the load's breath, the carry's pace, the time, the air. The fantasy is a bargain (spend to take); if the price could lie the bargain would be meaningless and the player would stop making it. If only the prize can lie, every take is also a bet on one's own perception, and the field-check ladder is the player buying information before or during the bet: Pillar 4's verification economy made physical. Each price channel is already truthful by owners' rules: the noise is Attention's (its world signs are the web's, not sanity's, ART 2.7), the hiss and room air are on the Graph's consequence channel (LSG:44), the breath comes from true `mass_g` (SE-28, IDB-22), the pace is the Controller's locked `V_CARRY`, and the beam is the truth anchor of the visual identity.

| Class | Tier | What it does to the sortie | Truth that remains |
|---|---|---|---|
| (b) false system state | **MVP** | A FAILED module's sprite or gauge reads alive (its lantern cannot: lamp brightness is on the Graph's closed consequence list, LSG:44): the *decide* step becomes a verification act. Cutting a module that was already dead is "the time the loot was garbage" (concept Key Dynamics). | Consequence-channel witnesses in its compartment (DA-34) and SCAN, the honest MVP instrument (BS-17). |
| (d) phantom salvage | VS-A (hooks now) | The thing on the floor is real but its sprite is a prize: the walk home becomes doubt. | Breath-step on true mass, SCAN, PALETTE leak, authored absence, the trip home (SN-21 ladder, BS-14); "drop the plate first". |
| (a) phantom entity | VS | A thing in the dark during the carry tempts a drop that was not needed. | The hallucination hides the body, never the trail; a real one leaves Pillar 1 and 2 footprints. A drop is free to retake when lit (BS-13), so a false alarm costs time, not the haul. |
| (c) crewmate as horror | VS-B | A crewmate hauling a module may render as a horror. | The human channel: their beam colour and shape and their voice. |

**MVP note.** The MVP ships class (b) only; the anchor moment above works with *zero* hallucination, and nothing in this GDD implies phantom salvage is in the MVP. The hooks are named; their acceptance is deferred (BS-14). **The `scr_a` moment is the MVP's taste of Pillar 4.** A player who cuts the dead scrubber without reading it has been fooled by nothing but their own hurry; the world told them it was dead; SCAN, or the woken bus, would have told them it was broken (BS-17 names which witnesses are of record and which only a SCAN or a woken bus can supply). It teaches the one lesson class (d) will need: *read before you take*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(a correct read of `scr_a` changes no outcome at the MVP (no destination), so as specced the beat is flavour and not yet a stake; see the Overview note and OQ-BS-3)*

### Reference techniques

| Reference | Adopt | Reject |
|---|---|---|
| **Project Zomboid** | "Every door is a decision" (ART 2.6); the carried object as a felt burden that changes how you move; death is a bitch (user decision). | The numeric weight readout, inventory grid, encumbrance moodle and mass-scaled speed. |
| **Hardspace: Shipbreaker** | Cutting is a careful act with physical consequences: the wrong cut vents and the vent is the lesson (stub hiss, `open_end` leak). | Per-piece cash value, quotas, freeform deconstruction of the hull (anti-pillar: not a freeform builder; no `remove_module`). |
| **Lethal Company** | The run back with hands full; dropping your scrap to survive is a real story; the ship is the only safety. | Sale value, quota pressure, mass that slows you, the comedic register, loot that respawns (P5). |
| **Barotrauma** | Crew retreat as a first-class action: leave the module, save the diver. | Item-wiring complexity and container inventories. |
| **Alien: Isolation** | Your own noise as the threat channel; stillness as a strategy. | The motion tracker (an Attention meter in another form: P3, AT-20). |
| **Pandorum** | The chest lamp, hands-free (user decision): carrying never blinds you. | Combat set-pieces. |
| **Saturation diving** (non-game) | "Bottom time": reserve (`RESERVE_S` 90 s [PLACEHOLDER]) and cell (`CELL_S` 1200 s [PLACEHOLDER]) as the sortie's physical budget; one lift per descent; the umbilical as the pod you must get back to. | A dive-computer readout (there is no sortie timer, BS-24). |
| **Opening a sealed tomb by one lamp** (non-game) | The read precedes the take: see before you touch. | Treasure framing: no reward glow (ART 4.2: "Reward is not a colour either"). |
| **Beyond the Aquila Rift** | The kind lie: the prize looks like what you hoped for (class d, VS-A). | Any lie about the price. |

### Tone and the anti-pillar guard rails

**Register:** quiet, procedural, physical; the voice of a salvage-dive logbook, not a loot screen. Verbs over nouns; costs stated as bodily or audible facts. Zero diegetic jokes: the dark comedy is emergent ("the time the loot was garbage") and belongs to the players' stories, not the game's voice. Use *take, cut free, lift, haul, the walk home, set down, leave it; kerf, stub, cap, hiss, the breath, the load; the web noticed, it leaned in; bottom time*. Avoid *loot (as in loot drop), reward, farm, harvest, grind; weight limit, encumbrance, capacity, inventory (outside the one conflict row that quotes them); flesh, gore, wet, organ (in any art, audio or player-facing text); aggro, threat level, detection meter; timer, countdown; spawn (for derelict A)*. These extend the stale-token lint (AC-BS-64).

The eleven guard rails (no combat; no progression from salvage; salvage is not construction; no economy; no readout of weight, value, condition or capacity; no sortie timer and no Attention meter; no forced script; no intentional PvP over salvage; the body metaphor never reaches the senses; no win condition in a haul; the price never lies) are stated once, as the last rule of Core Rules (BS-24), and apply to every section.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Format.* **BS-n — Name.** *Rule.* / *Why.* / *Pillar.* / *Decided.* A rule that states a mechanic ends in "(owner rule-id)"; a rule with no owner citation is a sortie-level composition rule (BS-2, BS-5, BS-8, BS-12, BS-13, BS-14, BS-15, BS-17, BS-19, BS-24). Citation forms: IA = interaction-system, SE = suit-and-equipment, IDB = item-and-module-database, MSA = modular-ship-assembly, DA = derelict-authoring-handmade-kits, LY = ship-section-and-layout-data-model, BDY = ship-body-and-interior-physics, AT = attention-system, SL = save-load-and-versioned-format, SN = sanity-and-hallucination-rules, HE = horror-entities, WM = web-map-and-transit, PC / PCI = player-controller-input, LSG / LS = life-support-graph, FT = failure-and-trail-system, EM = emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query, ART = the art bible, KRN = command-simulation-layer. **Every neighbour cited is Designed and locked *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the status header of each neighbour file was re-read on 2026-10-27, and Perception Filter, Adaptive Audio & Tells, HUD & Suit Layer, In-World Instruments, Chart Table and Debug Overlay are now Designed, Dependencies rows 17 and 17b)* except the Vertical Slice systems still undesigned (their contracts are provisional). Web Map & Transit's constants (`HOP_FUEL_ML`, `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML`, `HOP_LEG_TICKS`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS`, `RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML`, `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS`) are registry entries (source web-map-and-transit.md) marked [PLACEHOLDER].** Every number is an owner's, cited by registered name with its owner's tag; `[PLACEHOLDER]` marks the owner's placeholder wherever the value is used. "Decided. run ruling Rn" names the orchestrated run's stage rulings. The tag [PLACEHOLDER] marks each constant that the registry marks as a placeholder, and is repeated at first use per section and in every Tuning Knobs row; constants the registry holds as locked (for example `V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `V_CARRY`, `TICK_RATE`, `TILE_PX`, `TELL_MIN_TICKS`, `TELL_MAX_TICKS`, `REACH_PX`, `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` and the Save/Load intervals) carry no tag.

**BS-1 — Composition only: the ownership map and the zero footprint.** *Rule.* Boarding & Salvage registers **no state block, no step hook, no command type, no mutator, no read view, no notification and no registry constant**; it holds no scene-tree code (KRN CR-14, H10) and adds no engine surface. Its registry delta is `referenced_by` entries and design-time formulas only. Every sortie word has a designed owner; Boarding states how the loop *uses* it and never how it works:

| Sortie word | Owner and rule | What Boarding adds |
|---|---|---|
| enter | pod arrival at the `entry_room_key` cell (WM-15), dormant-to-loaded (DA-3, DA-6, WM-24) | the dock room is the retreat anchor (BS-3, BS-13) |
| listen | idle pushes no noise (PC-10); the Graph's consequence channel (LSG:44); Attention's pre-tell hull creaks in CALM (AT-26, quantised and floor-gated) and, during a TELL, the non-positional structure-borne tell bed (AT-15, AUD-4) | a stance, not a verb (BS-4) |
| light | chest lamp `toggle_beam` (SE-8..SE-10), `act_gate` (IA-6), `throw_glowstick` (IA-29), the exposure table (EM) | lit job = visible job (BS-5) |
| decide | nothing owns a readout (Pillar 1); evidence is the world (DA-34, ART 6.5) | world-only evidence (BS-6) |
| cut a module free | V8 `begin_cut` to M6 `salvage_node` (IA-25, LSG:185) | the cut as a priced, committing decision (BS-7) |
| cap or leave | `begin_cap` to M12 `cap_port` (IA-26, LSG:191) | the stub policy (BS-8) |
| pick up, carry, drop | `pickup`/`drop`/`loose[]` (IA-28), `carry_ref` and gait (PC-8, PC-9), the carried instance (SE-12, SE-13) | the carry-admitted set, doors, weight-as-body (BS-9..BS-11) |
| the yield | `salvage_yield` (IDB-17), deterministic | none |
| the hop | `pod_hop` (WM-8) | the cargo rule (BS-12) |
| install | `install_module` (MSA-26, MSA-27) | the precondition repeated (BS-18) |
| field checks | `field_check` (IA-30), `record_falsification` and the lure ladder (SN-17, SN-21), the weight cue (SE-28) | the procedure (BS-14) |
| under rising Attention | `push_stimulus` (IA-33), the integrator, tell window and abort (AT-3, AT-8, AT-10) | declared retreat conditions (BS-13) |
| loose items | placement DA-26, slots LY-22, holder and mechanism IA-28 | sortie-level rules only (BS-16) |

Repeated verbatim as the Suit asks (SE:471): **the loose-world list is not Boarding's; Interaction's IA-28 owns `loose[]`.** *Why.* A block would duplicate `loose[]` and `stubs[]` (IA-28), `carried` (SE-13), `carry_ref` (PC-8) and `pod_at` (WM) and break one-writer-per-fact (KRN H3); Derelict Authoring set the precedent (DA-1). *Engine.* `docs/engine-reference/godot/` has **no scripting, core or gameplay-logic module**; this domain (rule composition, integer arithmetic, data-driven content) has no engine file to cite, and `audio.md` informs only the event list in Visual/Audio. The 4.7 typed-return override risk (a handler base-class override now needs an explicit `return`) belongs to the Interaction and Assembly handlers, not here. No ADR of its own. *Pillar.* none directly; it guards KRN H3. *Decided.* run rulings R1, R30.

**BS-2 — The sortie is a sequence of owned steps, not a machine.** *Rule.* Boarding stores no sortie state and names none; the sequence reuses `loco_state` (PC), the Attention `phase` (AT) and `transit_state` (WM) by citation. The table is the design's spine: each step has an owner, a perceived beat, a cost and a failure. Every time is a **lower bound** while the tool-swap duration is unregistered (`equip_tool` has no equip time beyond the Controller's hand animation, SE-11; Sanity's `T_EQUIP` is carried as 0, SN OQ-SN-6).

| # | Step | Owner rule | The player perceives | Cost | What can go wrong |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Prepare at home | SE-11 (slots 1 cutter, 2 welder, 3 scanner, 4 suppressant; `START_EQUIPPED_SLOT` 2) | the tool in hand, the glowstick count on the suit's rail | the swap (`equip_tool`; `BUSY` while carrying or channelling) | the wrong tool is honest: `NO_TOOL` after the press (IA-5) |
| 2 | Board (hop out) | WM-8, WM-20 (fuel), WM-34 (leg) | the console press, the hush, a black window | `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] from home only, `HOP_LEG_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` | `FUEL_LOW`, `NOT_LIT`, `BERTH_PENDING`, `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED` (a defect, WM-15); `CAPACITY` is a preview of another system's hard runtime cap and is unreachable at MVP (WM-9) |
| 3 | Enter | WM-15, DA-6 | arrival one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop; the derelict dark except its one residual lamp | none | none authored at MVP |
| 4 | Listen | PC-10, LSG:44, AT-15, AT-26 | Attention's pre-tell hull creaks in CALM (AT-26, quantised and floor-gated) and, during a TELL, the non-positional structure-borne tell bed (AT-15, AUD-4); hiss, creak, the player's own breath | time; the sanity dwell `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` [PLACEHOLDER] (Sanity's, BS-20) | hurrying skips the only free information |
| 5 | Light | SE-8..SE-10, IA-6, IA-29 | the beam from the chest point; a stick lands lit | `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER] of beam; `GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` [PLACEHOLDER] sticks; each stick one `U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER] at landing (IA-29) | cell low, sticks spent; a lit job is a visible job |
| 6 | Decide | BS-6 | dust, trails, lantern, the stub it would leave, what home lacks | time | a wrong read installs a bad module, honestly (`condition_bp` carries, IDB-17) |
| 7 | Position and tool | IA-6, SE-11 | the tool at the grip, the beam on the node | walking at `V_WALK` (24 AU per tile) plus the swap | the node sits in a hazard room (DA-28) |
| 8 | Cut | IA-25 | a linear bar; the module stays; at completion a kerf, the yield loose on the deck, stubs hissing | `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]; `U_TOOL` [PLACEHOLDER] per accrued tick plus one `U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER] | abort set (IA-15), `BUSY` (IA-34), `NOT_SALVAGEABLE`, a tell |
| 9 | Cap or leave | IA-26, BS-8 | the hiss continues or ends | `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` per stub plus a welder swap | `INVALID_TARGET` if already capped; hands must be free |
| 10 | Pick up | IA-28 | the module leaves the floor; the load breath (SE-28); the one prompt `G . Drop` | a lit TAP; hands full from here | an unlit item cannot be taken; `BUSY` if already carrying |
| 11 | Carry to the dock | PC-8, IDB F-13 | gait forced to `V_CARRY`, no run; every prompt but `drop` gone | 48 ticks and 48 AU per tile, mass-independent | a tell or breach (BS-13); a closed door (BS-10); a hazard dwell |
| 12 | Hop home | WM-8 plus BS-12 | `IN_POD`, not drawn; `carry_ref` intact on arrival | `HOP_LEG_TICKS`; the return is prepaid (`RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` kept 0) | the console press while carrying, if the carry-admitted rows are refused (BS-9) |
| 13 | Install | MSA-26 | the module seated; the hardpoint arms | a TAP; chain M5, `release_carried`, `clear_carry_ref`, `sync_section` | no prompt at an incompatible hardpoint |
| 14 | Settle | SL-4, SL-5 | an autosave; the derelict stands as left (WM-28) | none | none |

*Why.* The five-minute loop is a sequence of costs, and a state machine would restate `loco_state`, the Attention `phase` and `transit_state`. *Pillar.* P3 (the cost per step is the design). *Decided.* run ruling R12.

**BS-3 — Enter: the dock is a threshold, not a safe room.** *Rule.* Arrival is at the authored `arrive_*` cell of the target's `entry_room_key` room, one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop (WM-15, DA-6); the derelict goes live in `pod_settle` and its roster `status` goes to BOARDED (WM-14, WM-28). Boarding states two things only. (a) The dock room is where the pod waits and is therefore **the retreat anchor** (BS-13). (b) Nothing in the dock says what is aboard: derelict A's dock holds no machinery (DA-2), and the first read is the world's. The pod console is an Interaction CONSOLE subject on the docked-podcraft prop (WM-8); its placement and facing are Layout's and Derelict Authoring's. Content constraint proposed to them (not data here): **the way home is in sight on arrival** — the console sits within two tiles of the arrival cell on the side the returning player faces, so a carrying actor (whose facing follows movement, PC-8) faces the console without turning and the forward beam lights it (ASK-BS-D2). Horror places nothing in salvage (HE:376, Designed). *Why.* Naming the dock as a threshold and not a refuge fixes the escape anchor in the player's mind without making the first room safe or saying what is aboard; a console in sight on arrival is the cheapest way to keep the one-press retreat honest with hands full. *Pillar.* P2 (the escape verb stays one press with hands full), P3. *Decided.* run rulings R12, R25.

**BS-4 — Listening is a stance, not a verb.** *Rule.* There is no `listen` action and no audibility boost. Standing still pushes no noise (PC-10: idle is 0) and lets Attention's excess decay by the integrator's own term (`attention_integrator`; see Formulas). What there is to hear is the Graph's acoustic classes — **hiss, creak and whistle at a descriptor's anchor** (the closed consequence list, LSG:44) — and Attention's pre-tell hull creaks in CALM (AT-26, quantised and floor-gated) and, during a TELL, the non-positional structure-borne tell bed (AT-15, AUD-4). Nothing in any channel shows the Attention level (AT-20). The hum of a running machine is an Audio class (FT:207, HUM_DROP) and is true-state audio in the consequence channel (LSG:976) but is not in LSG:44's enumerated list; Boarding uses it as true-state corroboration and does not make it the falsifier of record until the Graph rules (C-BS-4). Dwell has a sanity price owned by Sanity: `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` [PLACEHOLDER] per run while off the home body (SN, BS-20). *Why.* A `listen` hold that boosts the bed would be an Attention meter in audio form (AT-20, P3). *Pillar.* P3. *Decided.* run rulings R15, R29.

**BS-5 — Light is the price of a job.** *Rule.* Every channelled or object-addressed sortie verb (`begin_cut`, `begin_cap`, `field_check`, `pickup`, `install_module`) is lit-only: `act_gate(o) = in_reach(o) AND lit(o)` (IA-6). The exceptions are exactly `drop` of the carried module, `toggle_beam` and the inspect card of an already-lit instrument; `throw_glowstick` is an actor verb that needs no lit target (IA-6, IA-29). **Boarding adds no dark exemption for extraction**: a dark cut is impossible, so the job always shows in the web's exposure (EM). Lighting a job site costs the beam (`CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER], Suit) or one thrown stick (`GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` [PLACEHOLDER]; `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER]; `THROW_RANGE_TILES` [PLACEHOLDER]; the stick lights at landing, never before, IA-29) and pushes one `U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER] 180 AU at landing (IA-29, IA-33), the same report as a completed cut, so a stick buys light with noise and the beam buys it with cell. Throwing is `BUSY` while carrying, so a drop zone is lit **before** the pickup, not after. *Why.* "Unlit objects can be worked on" would make darkness a free Attention dodge (IA-6). *Pillar.* P3 (the system test: an extraction that can be done dark and free vs one that must be lit and noisy, lit and noisy). *Decided.* run ruling R12. The one stated exemption proposed for the console is a separate, routed ask (BS-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(refused by Interaction, IA-6; the mechanism is a USER DECISION, OQ-BS-4)*.

**BS-6 — Decide uses the world only.** *Rule.* The evidence for "cut this?" is: the module's own sprite and dialect; the stub it would leave (IDB-7, per def: a scrubber one O2 stub, a coolant pump two, power-only modules none); the consequence-channel witnesses in its compartment (trail, lantern, lamp brightness, draught, the room's O2 and temperature as the body feels them, LSG:44); undisturbed dust as supporting verdict grammar (ART 6.5); and what the home ship visibly lacks. **There is no value tag, rating, condition bar, rarity, loot glow or inspect-card condition.** The inspect card is module-body and can lie (LS-22); it is live at MVP (In-World Instruments IW-21), so it is a corruptible read and never a falsifier of record. *Why.* ART 4.2: "Reward is not a colour either: salvage is Graphite plus its loop hue"; a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe is the Pillar 1 test, and the pipe wins. *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* run ruling R9.

**BS-7 — The cut is a priced, committing decision.** *Rule.* `begin_cut` (cutter in hand, slot 1) is a CHANNEL of `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] whose effect lands at completion only; an abort leaves the module in place and the ledger keeps the partial for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] (IA-25, IA-15, IA-16). At completion Interaction runs M6 `salvage_node`, yields one LOOSE_WORLD item per yielded instance at the node anchor, **never auto-carried**, and registers one STUB per `open_end` fluid port (IA-25). The web hears it: `U_TOOL` per accrued tick plus one-shot `U_LOUD` (IA-33; units are Attention's). A cut is an EXOGENOUS act and bypasses the Graph's fairness gate and any need for a tell (LS-13). **Salvaging the running machine is legal**: the island re-solves on the next run and its dependants go STARVE with HUM_DROP descriptors (LSG:773, "the hum of the thing being cut is its warning" — true-state audio, LSG:976, used as corroboration and not yet the falsifier of record, C-BS-4); there is no special case. One job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`: a second seat on the same node is `BUSY` (IA-34). A cut begun at tell entry completes inside any tell a player meets (`SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` is well under the played draw `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 [PLACEHOLDER] to `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 [PLACEHOLDER], AT-8) but leaves the remainder for the carry. In the shortest tell a player meets (`TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 [PLACEHOLDER], AT-8), a carry begun at tell entry covers at most 18 tiles (900/48), and a cut begun at tell entry leaves 660 ticks, which is 13 tiles (`tell_window_fit`, Formulas). At the 600-tick fixture floor (`TELL_MIN_TICKS`, reachable only through `attention_arm_tell`, AT-21, and fixtures) these are 12 and 7. The real decision of the sortie is *weigh the module against the tell you may be buying*. There is no auto-cap, no auto-pickup and no required order. *Why.* The cut is where the player spends and where they cannot un-spend. An abort keeps the partial for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] (IA-15, IA-16), so stopping still long enough to abort a tell (`ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], AT-10) keeps the cut if it is resumed within 10 s; the margin is thin, because the stillness that aborts a tell itself takes about 594 ticks from an entry at the threshold and about 630 from the level ceiling (`tell_window_fit`, Formulas), so the partial survives only an immediate resume at the lowest entry levels. *Pillar.* P3, P1. *Decided.* run rulings R9, R12.

**BS-8 — Cap or leave: optional and priced.** *Rule.* A cut leaves one STUB per `open_end` fluid port; `begin_cap` (welder in hand, `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, ledger-resumable, `INVALID_TARGET` if already capped) ends it; an uncapped stub hisses forever and **no timer forces anything** (IA-26, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`, LSG:772). Power-only modules (heater, breaker junction, lamp, door motor, battery) leave no stub, so the reference payoff `htr_a` never hisses (IDB-7). Capping needs the welder in hand and hands free (`equip_tool` and every other `interact` are `BUSY` while carrying, SE-11, PC-8), so the natural order is cut, **cap, then pick up**, which follows from "never auto-carried" and not from a rule. The policy the player learns from the world: **cap what will be yours; leave what you are leaving.** A departed derelict goes dormant and its state stands still (LS-17, WM-24), so the leave cost within one sortie is the standing hiss (a consequence-channel truth, P1), the gas lost while the player lingers aboard, and what the next sortie finds; capping pays for the **weld donor** and for the player's comfort. If the leave cost is negligible within one sortie on the MVP roster, that is a **finding recorded and not repaired** (Formulas, `cap_versus_leave`) — no pressure is invented to make capping matter. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the Graph has since answered ASK-BS-G1(b), LSG:794: a welded section's stub leaks from the home network once the seam valve is opened, so capping does pay for the weld donor; on a derelict you leave, capping is still never the better trade at the MVP, which is why cap-or-leave is not a design-test row)* *Why.* A required cap would be a hidden gate (IA-26); an auto-cap on pickup would delete Pillar 1's consequence. *Pillar.* P1 (the hiss is truth), P5. *Decided.* run ruling R14.

**BS-9 — Pick up and carry: never auto-carried; the carry-admitted set is closed.** *Rule.* A yield is picked up by a lit TAP (`pickup`, IA-28): a CARRIED-class def needs hands free (no carried instance; `BUSY` if one is held), moves LOOSE_WORLD to CARRIED, sets `carry_ref` through the Controller's `set_carry_ref` and the instance through Suit's `set_carried` (IA-28, SE-13). **Closing IA Open Question 17: a yield is never auto-carried**, confirming IA-25's locked text, so the lit-pickup cost and cap-before-carry sequencing stand. While carrying: gait is `V_CARRY`, no run (PC-8); stagger keeps the module and downed drops it (PC-8, PC-9); the **chest lamp stays available** (user decision 2026-10-26; PC-8's "beam off while carrying" is a Controller playtest lever, not reopened here); `throw_glowstick` and `equip_tool` are `BUSY` (IA-29, SE-11). **The admitted interactions while carrying are exactly three: `drop` (IA-28); `install_module` on a HARDPOINT whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried def (MSA-26, carve-out C1, verbatim: "while carrying, `interact` on a HARDPOINT subject whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def is admitted, alongside `drop`"); and `pod_hop` on the pod CONSOLE (WM-8; PCI:694 "`carry_ref` is kept and the module travels with the pod (Boarding & Salvage's rule)").** `toggle_beam` stays available as an actor verb. Every other world target stays prompt-less and `BUSY` (IA-5). The collision with IA-5, IA-28 and V-13 ("every `interact` mode except `drop` is `BUSY`") and PC-8 was real and is closed (C-BS-1, ASK-BS-I1 answered): Interaction adds the CONSOLE `pod_hop` and HARDPOINT `install_module` rows with `admitted_while_carrying` true (registry: `admitted_while_carrying`; IA-2, IA-28); `berth` and `unberth` are not carry-admitted; the wording refreshes to PC-8, IA-5, IA-28 and V-13 stay with the Controller and Interaction (ASK-BS-C1). *Fallback, written as an edge case and not as a design, and **not shippable for the MVP**:* if the console row is refused, the module is dropped before the press and stays aboard; the carry-home branch is lost. That removes the loop the MVP exists to test (cut a module free, carry it home under rising Attention, install it), so the producer must treat a refusal of either row (the CONSOLE or the HARDPOINT) as a **stop**, not a degrade: the same loss follows from a refused HARDPOINT row, which removes the carried install. *Why.* PCI:694 and WM:414 already grant the console press while carrying and MSA-26 carve-out C1 the seated install, so a refusal is a cross-GDD conflict and not a Boarding degrade; the collision was wording plus two missing rows, which Interaction has since added (ASK-BS-I1 answered, OQ-BS-5 closed; formerly a blocking dependency), so all three carry-admitted verbs have a declaration. *Pillar.* P5 (the haul home is the loop), P2 (the escape verb stays one press). *Decided.* run rulings R3, R7, R8.

**BS-10 — Doors are the carry's terrain.** *Rule.* While carrying, doors are inert: the player may pass an OPEN door and may not operate a CLOSED one (IA-5, PC-8). A door met CLOSED with a module in hand is solved by `drop`, then V4 `set_opening` (a bare-hand TAP of `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], QUIET, IA-24), then a lit `pickup`; no extra rule is added. The doctrine is **open on the way in, carry through on the way out**. A door the player closed behind them as a containment tool (a legal Pillar 3 act) is their own cost on the way out. Content constraint (not a lint): no SYSTEM-origin door close on a carry route at MVP, because a carrier cannot reopen it and IA-5 would trap them (ASK-BS-I2); a closing door whose leaf overlaps a foot box retries each tick and lands on the first clear tick (IA-24). Admitting V4 while carrying would be a fourth carve-out and is recorded as an Open Question (OQ-BS-2), not adopted. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Costs the doctrine used to leave out.** (1) *Preconditions.* The reference figures (D.1) assume `door_airlock_n` already OPEN (the player opened it on the way out) and `door_cargo_s` CLOSED at home, and both A doors CLOSED at the start; a door found otherwise changes `n_closed`, and AC-BS-6 fixes the starting state. (2) *The cooled spine.* Leaving `door_a_spine_machine` open on the way in mixes lethal cold into `a_spine` by `K_OPEN` conduction, and the carry back through it is the cost; it is Derelict Authoring's open-door projection (DA D.18), cited in D.4 and not recomputed. (3) *Discoverability.* The drop, open, re-take trick has a diegetic cue (the door's own prompt returns on the `drop` frame, Visual/Audio step 11) and a playtest row (X-BS-12, AC-BS-124); the cold that the player perceives before the crossing is X-BS-13 (AC-BS-125); the first-door choice and the leading element that pulls toward `scr_a` are X-BS-14 (AC-BS-126, a sightline pass with Derelict Authoring). *Why.* The drop-open-pickup cycle costs roughly half a second of door time plus two TAPs, is Pillar 3-positive and needs no new carve-out. *Pillar.* P3. *Decided.* run ruling R3 (level-designer LD-R4).

**BS-11 — Weight is the body, never a number.** *Rule.* Mass never changes `V_CARRY` or the carry noise (IDB-21, `NOISE_PER_TICK` carry 1 [PLACEHOLDER]); haul cost per tile is identical for a 90 kg cell and a 6 kg core (IDB F-13). The only weight surfaces are the Suit's body cue, `cue_load = max(carried_class, stowed_class)` (SE-28, breath with a load signature, tools excluded), and structure: one carried instance, hands full, and the HEAVY `part_hull_plate` with `stack_max` 1. A stowed HEAVY masks every carried class (SE-28, SE OQ-8; `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS` [PLACEHOLDER] MEDIUM). **No pack capacity, encumbrance meter, pod scale or weight number is added** (SE-12 "no pack capacity and no weight limit"; IDB-21). *Why.* A weight number is an inventory screen by another name (SE-12). *Pillar.* P1. *Decided.* run ruling R4.

**BS-12 — The pod-hop cargo rule.** *Rule.* **What crosses with the pod is what the seated crew holds:** per seat one CARRIED instance (`carry_ref`, unchanged — instance-table row 12, IDB:450) plus the STOWED stacks (SE-12). **Pod capacity is the number of seats** (`seat_pod_1`, one at MVP, DA-3, WM-5; Boarding's structural rule); Web Map's `CAPACITY` reason is another system's runtime cap, unreachable at MVP (WM-9), and never a cargo or mass reason. **The bound on weight is structural** — one carried module, hands full, no run, every other verb `BUSY`, the HEAVY stack cap, the body cue — never a number, never a pod scale, never a refusal reason that reads module-body state (IA-11). No module rides unaccompanied; a module left in a dock room stays aboard as left (WM-28). `pod_hop` aborts any open channel (`ABORT(BODY)`) and a cut ledger older than `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] is gone on return (IA-40, IA-16). On arrival the actor is IDLE with `carry_ref` intact, then CARRY_WALK on the first non-NONE octant (PCI:694). **One module per hop at MVP**, so a second useful module costs a second `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] payment (WM-20). At Web Map's published figures that payment is 2 percent of the home tank, so fuel is a campaign clock shared with the reactor and not the cap on a haul (design-time, `modules_per_fuel`, Formulas; C-BS-14); the pressure on the haul is the hands, the walk and the tell. The routed thread "pod capacity and weight limit the haul" is thereby honoured structurally and not as a number, which **conflicts in letter** with SE-12 and IDB-21 and is flagged for creative-director review (C-BS-2, resolved for MVP by creative-director, 2026-10-26; revisit at the kill/continue playtest). Option B (a cargo cradle with a class-point ceiling) is the recorded Vertical Slice upgrade path with its asks pre-listed (Open Questions OQ-BS-1); any future cradle must be a visible physical surface the player loads by hand, and any refusal a world consequence (the pod visibly will not seal), never a text reason naming mass. *Why.* Zero new machinery, no oracle, and the concept loop is "find one salvageable system". *Pillar.* P1 (no readout), P3 (hands-full is the cost). *Decided.* run ruling R4; creative-director ruling D-CD-2.

**BS-13 — Retreat and abandon are legal, free and deliberate.** *Rule.* The sortie's declared breach-retreat conditions (the ask at AT:380):

| # | Condition | Effect | Owner rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| a | Leave the locus body by any route, normally the dock plus the pod console | the hop is **always allowed** during a TELL or BREACH (the escape verb) | WM-26 |
| b | Departure from a non-home locus body | ends a BREACH when the last recorded occupant leaves; a hop does not interrupt a TELL | AT-22 |
| c | Stillness and darkness | a TELL aborts when `L < theta_HOLD` for `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | AT-10 |
| d | `drop` the carried module, anywhere | legal, free, exempt from the lit rule; the actor then runs at `V_RUN` 3 vs `V_CARRY` 1 | IA-28, IA-6, PC-8 |
| e | A downed actor | drops the module (the Controller clears `carry_ref`; Interaction's hook runs at divisor 1 after the Controller's, so the module is a loose item **within one tick**, IA-28) | PC-9, SE-13 |

An abandoned module persists in `loose[]` as left and the derelict persists as left (WM-24, WM-28, IA-28); dropped in the dark it cannot be re-taken until something lights it (IA-28). **There is no forced drop, no hop refusal with cargo, and no tool kills a horror**: tools repel and distract and nothing is a weapon (anti-pillar). A horror met during a carry is answered by retreat, stillness, darkness or a repel tool; Horror places nothing in salvage and has no edge to author (HE:376, Designed). Every sortie step from the cut to the hop (steps 8 to 12) therefore has a legal exit. *Why.* A carried module that cannot be abandoned would turn a tell into a sentence. *Pillar.* P2, P3; anti-pillar (not a combat game). *Decided.* run rulings R13, R25. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Gating note: BS-13 opens no story yet.** The conditions are legal as written, but the decision they exist to serve (keep walking, or set it down) is not live at the current numbers: the reference carry always fits the tell, the tell does not open from the sortie's own noise, and freezing after the cut is a free third option (Player Fantasy note; D.2; D.3). Until the ruling of OQ-BS-27 shows, on `REF_GOLDEN`, a tell that is live and caused by the take, no story is opened for BS-13's decision-bearing rows or for the tell acceptance rows that need a sortie-produced tell (AC-BS-72, AC-BS-122, AC-BS-123, the CALM clause of AC-BS-14b). The fixture-armed mechanics rows (AC-BS-17, 18, 34, 35, 87, 93) test Attention's and Web Map's owned behaviour with `attention_arm_tell` and stay open as contract tests.

**BS-14 — Field checks: the procedure and the ladder (class (d) hooks).** *Rule.* Interaction owns the `field_check` channel (IA-30); Sanity owns lure data, `lures[]`, the ledger and `record_falsification(player_id, check_kind, subject_ref, outcome)` (SN-17, Class B); the Item DB owns true mass and the junk defs (IDB-22, VS-A). **Boarding owns the procedure**: when a crew runs the ladder during a sortie, its preconditions, and what each rung costs in the sortie's budget. The ladder is SN-21's, cheapest first:

| Rung | Instrument | Cost inside the sortie | Tier | Precondition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The **weight breath-step**: pick it up and feel `cue_load` (SE-28) | the pickup, then `drop` if suspect; no tool, no channel | MVP on real modules and the hull plate; lures VS-A | the stowed class is below the carried class under test (`STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS` [PLACEHOLDER]): **drop the plate first** |
| 2 | **SCAN** (`check_kind` SCAN) | `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], lit, scanner in hand (slot 3), plus the swap | MVP (the scanner is an honest anchor, SN-19) | scanner held; hands free |
| 3 | **PALETTE** (the violet or flesh leak on the sprite) | sight only | VS-A | the Perception Filter (Designed) |
| 4 | The **authored-absence read**: undisturbed dust, a missing verdict trail, a dried trail on a module's own fitting (DA:1189; FT:71 "a candidate field check, never a guarantee") | walking and looking | MVP | exists only where Derelict Authoring placed a dead fault |
| 5 | **The trip home** | the dearest | MVP | none |

A rung can fail to *apply* (a stowed HEAVY, no scanner, no authored trail) but never lies in the MVP; no result is stored as truth about a lie in `interaction.state` (IA-30). The design test is Sanity's `C_check <= 0.05 x T_home` (SN-21 and the class (d) design test of SN D.7, where `T_home` is "the one-way trip home with the item"), and the break-even walk-versus-scan distance `d*` is 5 tiles (SN-18, AC-SN-70); whether SCAN passes the test on the MVP routes is a finding in Formulas (`check_cost_ratio`), not a rule. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **SCAN is an explicit MVP exception to SN-21 and SN D.7's 5 percent test**: it passes on no MVP route (0.250 to 0.056), class (d) ships no junk for it to disprove, and the exception lapses at VS-A, where either the junk placement keeps `T_home` at least 3120 ticks (D.7) or `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` is bounded at 66 or less (`C_check` = 36 + 66 = 102 and `20 * 102` = 2040 is at most the 2055 ticks of reading (c) for `htr_a`; it still fails readings (a) and (b)). IA-30 reserves `check_kind` HEFT (the lift-and-weigh) and PALETTE for VS-A; at MVP rung 1 is perception only (SE-28) and records nothing, and the recorded form is HEFT at VS-A. **"Drop the plate first" is not yet performable**: the plate is a STOWED stack, `drop` (IA-28) releases only the carried instance, and no in-world way to shed a stowed stack is written (ASK-BS-S1); until one is, the ladder skips rung 1 and uses SCAN or the trip home. MVP exercises: the breath-step on real carried modules and the plate, SCAN, and the absence read on `scr_a` (BS-17). Class (d) itself is VS-A; its acceptance rows are **deferred**, blockers "junk defs (IDB-22) + the Perception Filter + SN-21", and the blind two-clip audio identification test of the load cue (SE RK-2, at least 90 %) is a precondition of the VS-A breath-step. *Why.* The trip home is the fallback, not the only reveal (Pillar 4(d)). *Pillar.* P4. *Decided.* run ruling R17.

**BS-15 — The lie is in the prize, never in the price.** *Rule.* A hallucination may lie about **what** a module is or **whether** it is worth taking. It may never lie about **what taking it costs**: the cut's noise, the stub's hiss, the load's breath, the carry's pace, the time, the air. Each price channel is truthful by its owner's rule: noise is Attention's (IA-33, ART 2.7), hiss and room air are on the Graph's closed consequence list (LSG:44), the breath comes from true `mass_g` (SE-28, IDB-22), the pace is `V_CARRY` (PC-8), the beam is the truth anchor (EM). A phantom has no subject and answers like an unlit object (EM-21, IA-13), so reaching for one is not a free oracle. The MVP ships class (b) only. *Why.* If the price could lie the bargain is meaningless and the player stops making it; if only the prize can lie, every take is a bet on one's own perception and the ladder is how it is bought down. *Pillar.* P4, P1. *Decided.* creative-director guard rail G-11.

**BS-16 — The faucet is finite and persists as left.** *Rule.* Salvage is the only faucet (IDB-29): the authored module-node list (A 18 module nodes, B 22, home 26, DA-21), the seeded loose ledger (10 placements, DA-26) and `salvage_yield` (deterministic, no RNG, salvage-once, IDB-17). A SALVAGED slot is a tombstone and stays so (LSG:243). A left derelict stands exactly as left (WM-24, WM-28). The remaining salvage in a room is read from the room (the empty pedestal rect, LY-20; the scorched kerf; hissing or capped stubs; an empty shelf), never from a count (ART 7.4). Repeated as Derelict Authoring asks (DA:1239): **the faucet is the authored node list and loose placements; the ledger is finite; and a cheap field read exists for a failed module** (BS-14, BS-17). Sortie-level loose-item rules only: an abandoned item persists (BS-13); a yield appears at the node anchor; items are section-local, so a weld or detach moves nothing (LY-22). Compartment-level "first entry" knowledge is **not** Boarding's and is declined at MVP: Web Map keeps the derelict level (`FIRST_BOARDED`, WM-28), Layout keeps only `room_at`, and the owner is the Vertical Slice Knowledge Codex's PROFILE-scoped data (answering LY Open Question 12). *Why.* A faucet the player can read from the room and cannot refill is what makes scarcity push inward; a count, a first-entry flag or any regrowth would each be a meter or a farm. *Pillar.* P5 (a finite persisting list vs a refillable crate, finite). *Decided.* run rulings R11, R20.

**BS-17 — The taught payoff of sortie A is `htr_a`; `scr_a` is optional restraint.** *Rule.* The **reference first sortie** cuts `htr_a` (MEDIUM 12000 g, power-only so no stub, in `a_machine`) and installs it in the vacant `hp_cargo_heater`, which arms PWR-OVL at home (MSA:28, DA-12, DA:855): a consequence the player caused. **`a_machine` is the one lethal-cold room**, so the reference sortie is a *hazard* sortie: the carried round trip through an unbreathable stretch and the hazard crossing are bounded by Derelict Authoring's lints (BS-20) and the room's tell is the frost creeping over the sill of the closed door before it is opened. The dead scrubber `scr_a` (FAILED, `hp` 2000, HEAVY 40000 g, `a_life`) is a legal cut whose carry home has **no MVP destination** (no vacant scrubber hardpoint, no `part_machine_kit` placed — DA OQ-DA-12 — and no `remove_module`, MSA-28), so it is kept as the honest "the time the loot was garbage" beat (concept Key Dynamics) and as Derelict Authoring's *quiet alternative* (cut it and leave; DA:1157). This GDD does **not** script a 40 kg carry to nowhere as the first or the reference cut (IDB-14). *Falsifier of record for `scr_a` before the cut* (DA OQ-V10): **only what is on the Graph's closed consequence list** — lamp brightness (its NODE_LANTERN dark, the lamp-free neighbour branch, DA-24, DA:1445), the room's own O2 and temperature as the body feels them, draught — plus **SCAN as the priced second instrument**. The cut's missing hum is true-state audio in the consequence channel (LSG:976) but is not in LSG:44's enumerated list; Boarding uses it as true-state corroboration and does not make it the falsifier of record until the Graph rules (C-BS-4). On A's dead bus nothing hums, so the hum, like the lantern, falsifies 'running' only; the dead-bus analysis is unchanged; undisturbed dust is supporting verdict grammar (ART 6.5). **Weight cannot discriminate**: `scr_a` has the same def mass, 40000 g, as a healthy scrubber. **On a dead bus the closed-list witnesses falsify "running" only**: on A's dead bus no machine runs, so every lantern is dark, `scr_a`'s and `pump_a`'s alike (DA-24), and the room's O2 and temperature cannot tell an unpowered healthy scrubber from a FAILED one; health is discriminated by SCAN, or by waking the bus with the spare breaker (DA-25, AC-DA-111), after which `pump_a`'s lantern lights and `scr_a`'s stays dark. *Conditions (creative-director D-CD-3):* the beat is honest only if the falsifier works in playtest (playtest target X-BS-3, Game Feel and AC-BS-71); if it does not, the fallback is the content route (a vacant scrubber hardpoint or a placed kit, owners Derelict Authoring and Assembly). Conflicts routed: C-BS-3 (LS-26 "first V8" names `scr_a`), C-BS-4, C-BS-6 (DA playtest target X-DA-8). *Pillar.* P1 (no payoff-less carry is taught), P4, P5. *Decided.* run rulings R10, R18; creative-director D-CD-3.

**BS-18 — Install at home: the precondition repeated.** *Rule.* `install_module {hardpoint_ref}` is Assembly's PLAYER TAP and its prompt exists iff the seat carries an instance whose def is in `compatible_defs(h)` for the focused HARDPOINT (immediate, never an oracle); the chain is M5 `install_module`, `release_carried`, `clear_carry_ref`, `sync_section` (MSA-26, MSA-27). `condition_bp` carries through, so installing a FAILED-hp instance installs a FAILED module (IDB-17, IDB-18). There is **no `remove_module`** in the MVP: V8 salvage is the only way a module leaves INSTALLED, so the choice at the hardpoint is final (MSA-28). The Item DB's `T_cycle` — **cut + carry + `cap_port` on the stubs + install** (Interaction's and Assembly's verbs) — is the cost of one salvage-then-reinstall (IDB F-15); repeated as Assembly asks (MSA T12): the install precondition is *carried, compatible per `compatible_defs(h)`, at a hardpoint*. The patch cap and decay clock persist through salvage and reinstall (IDB-18, provisional on a Graph ruling, IDB OQ-10). The welder and section cut cannot run while carrying (MSA-26). *Why.* The install is the one place where the haul becomes the ship, and a seat with no take-back is what makes the take at the derelict a commitment; repeating the precondition keeps the sortie's last step from being read as inventory management. *Pillar.* P5; anti-pillar (not a freeform builder). *Decided.* run ruling R3.

**BS-19 — A dark dock must never be fatal: the hop-availability invariant.** *Rule.* **No sortie state exists in which the hop is unavailable for want of light alone.** `pod_hop` needs the CONSOLE lit and in reach (WM-8, IA-6; `NOT_LIT`), derelict dock fixtures are dark (DA authored), the beam is finite (`CELL_S`), and throwing a stick is `BUSY` while carrying: a player who has spent the cell, holds no stick and stands in a dark dock can neither press the console nor re-take a dropped module, which is a soft-lock, and Pillar 5 says failure costs ship and body, never a soft-lock. Existing mitigations narrow it: `a_dock` has a two-stick shelf (`ls_a_dock_shelf`, `cons_glowstick` stack 2), the home `airlock` a four-stick "pod-hop supply" (`ls_air_locker`, DA-26), and a carrier can still `drop`, throw a stick (one LOUD report at landing, IA-29) and re-take the module under its light (IA-29 is `BUSY` while carrying, channelling, staggered or downed, so it is free once the module is dropped). The strand needs all three at once: cell spent, zero sticks and an unlit console. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **It is not a corner case. It is the expected end of a long lit session, and the earlier framing rested on comparing the cell with one sortie.** The right comparison is the campaign (D.10): the reference line spends 17 permille of `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER] 1200 s, but a survey-style sortie lit for 300 s spends 250 permille, so the cell is gone in about 4 survey sorties; the MVP has no refill (V7 `top_up` is pending LS-26 and is a user question there, IA-9, Graph Open Question 20; `CELL_ENABLED` 0 is Suit's data-only fallback); and sticks are finite (the authored ledger on the A route offers the start kit of 2, the `a_dock` shelf of 2 and the home airlock locker of 4; each lights 150 s, `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` [PLACEHOLDER]; B's shelves are not counted). A lit-beam player who keeps playing therefore arrives at a spent cell, no sticks and a dark derelict dock, and under this rule that state stops the slice. It is a true soft-lock, because the dock is survivable (`a_dock` `T0` -6000 mC), so no death and no restore ever fires. **The invariant is BLOCKING for the implementation of this system** (stage-12 ruling after the CD-GDD-ALIGN concern: P5 says failure costs ship and body and never a soft-lock, and this strand fires no death and no restore, which breaks the failure contract more directly than any death does). Its acceptance rows are AC-BS-92 (a bounded reachability test over the matrix and the campaign-length strand cell), AC-BS-51a and AC-BS-51b: the sortie is not implementation-complete, and the slice does not ship, while the strand cell of AC-BS-92 fails; no sign-off replaces them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Status of the mechanism: none is adopted, and both owners have answered.** Interaction refused the lit exemption (IA-6 and Open Question 31: a fourth lit exception needs a PC-6 amendment by the Controller owner and the user's ruling that the stranding guarantee outranks the lit-only rule, P3). Web Map declined the stronger lint (OQ-WM-38 (a): an authored self-powered dock light is ruled out by its own answer to ASK-BS-W2). Web Map's text (WM-8, E-16, AC-WM-10, AC-WM-88) still reads `pod_hop` as exempt "provisional — Interaction", which Interaction's refusal makes stale (cross-GDD note). The fallback lint `DOCK_CONSOLE_DARK_NO_STICK` as worded cannot close the strand: it requires one authored loose glowstick per derelict dock, a loose stick is unlit until thrown (IA-29) and `pickup` is lit-only (BS-5), so a player with a spent cell cannot take it; it has the shape of the `a_dock` shelf, which narrows the strand and does not close it. **The invariant is therefore unmet by design today. The choice of mechanism and of cell policy is a USER DECISION (OQ-BS-4); this GDD does not pick one.** The candidates, each judged against the same bounded test (AC-BS-92):

| Mechanism | What it is | Cost or risk | Decides |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** (recommended) | A lit exemption in IA-6 for `pod_hop` on the CONSOLE only; the reach test is unchanged; `berth` and `unberth` stay lit-gated | A PC-6 amendment and the user's ruling that the stranding guarantee outranks lit-only for this one verb; no fixture cost; keeps the visible-unknown lint intact | user, Controller, Interaction, Web Map |
| **M2** | A pod-owned console lamp: a lamp that belongs to the player's pod, sits off the derelict bus and is always lit at a docked console | Costed in D.2 as one more lit fixture on the exposure ledger of every sortie; needs an Emitter State row and art; must not read as a second unknown lamp (DA-27) | user, Emitter State, Web Map, Derelict Authoring |
| **M3** | A reserved lamp charge that only the console press can spend | A new reserved slice of the cell in Suit's accounting; the world must show it (a dimmer beam when only the reserve remains) or P1 is broken | user, Suit, Interaction |
| Rejected | Allowing `throw_glowstick` while carrying | Removes the BS-5 cost of lighting the drop zone before the pickup | none |

The mechanism is half of the ruling; the other half is the **cell policy**: promote V7 `top_up` into the MVP (the Graph's LS-26 amendment), add a home-airlock recharge, or keep the cell non-renewable on purpose. The policy sets how soon a lit-beam player meets the dark dock (D.10). If the user rules that the strand is an accepted consequence, that is a change to Pillar 5's failure contract and not a Boarding waiver, and it is recorded as such in OQ-BS-4. *Pillar.* P5, P2. *Decided.* run ruling R3 (level-designer LD-R6); the stage-12 CD-GDD-ALIGN revision made the invariant, and not the mechanism, binding; the mechanism is OQ-BS-4.

**BS-20 — Content-side sortie constraints are citations; one design target.** *Rule.* These Derelict Authoring bounds are lint-backed facts Boarding relies on and re-checks across **all five loaded sets** S0 to S4 and both sortie orders (never only the first sortie): a carried round trip to the farthest salvage through an unbreathable stretch of at most 56 tiles (44.8 s at `V_CARRY`); a hazard crossing of at most 7 tiles of lethal cold (5.6 s); `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX` 1800, 600 when carrying (the endogenous-fault escape bound, DA-29, a different question from the module-saving bound); 18 / 22 module nodes (A / B); five loose parts; the loose ledger (DA-28, DA-29, DA-21, DA-25, DA-26, DA-32). **Design target, not a lint and not registered:** a first sortie to A completes cut and carry-home in about five minutes of *player time* (concept Core Loop; measured as W_LOOP, home console press to install, with its W_ABOARD part capped below Sanity's stage-1 point, D.1 and D.8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* with `RESERVE_S` [PLACEHOLDER] and `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER] sufficient. The route itself is a minute-scale lower bound; the five minutes are listening, lighting, deciding and door choices, so nobody tunes the route up to hit five minutes by distance (`V_CARRY` is locked). Sanity's dwell cost is cited and not set: `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` [PLACEHOLDER] (SN). **Subject budget:** Boarding adds no subject beyond the drops `P <= PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` and forbids any rule here that seeds more loose items; the post-weld sortie S4 `{home+B, A}` already exceeds `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` by 24 (144 vs 120, DA OQ-DA-20, owned by Derelict Authoring and Interaction, C-BS-7). Node anchors are not published by Derelict Authoring, so every route figure in this GDD is marked as assumed and deferred to Layout's `route_length_px` (ASK-BS-D1). *Why.* The authored bounds are what keep a carried return survivable and a retreat legal across every loaded set; citing them and minting no lint of Boarding's own keeps Derelict Authoring the one owner of the geometry, while the design target names only the felt length. *Pillar.* P2, P5. *Decided.* run rulings R23, R24, R29.

**BS-21 — Autosave after a cut.** *Rule.* "Section extraction" in SL-4 is **Assembly's detach** (BDY:341) and is already served by MSA-33's derived read `last_shape_change_T()`, polled by Save/Load; Boarding neither owns nor names it. For a **module** cut, a completed V8 should also shorten the loss window, following the same pattern: Interaction publishes a pure derived read of the last salvage-completion tick (the read `last_salvage_T()` (registry: `last_salvage_T`); **Boarding mints no name**, which is this GDD's answer to Save/Load OQ-24), Save/Load polls it under SL-5's gate and `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS`, and `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` guards a doomed save (SL-4, SL-5, SL-7, SL-8). A tell defers through SL-5 soft row (b) (provisional; an absent Attention view counts as open and logs `GATE_INPUT_ABSENT`); a channel, stagger or transit defers through `GATE_SOFT_STATES`; DOWNED and DEAD are hard. `CARRY_WALK` is not a gate state, so an autosave may land mid-carry; a cut's SEVERED descriptors are EXOGENOUS and open no row (a) window. The read therefore cannot trap the player in a doomed state. The brief's push-event idea is not adopted (Boarding cannot emit one, BS-1). *Why.* The loss ceiling (5.5 minutes) equals a whole sortie. *Pillar.* P5 (loss is not total). *Decided.* run ruling R6; ASK-BS-I4, ASK-BS-L1.

**BS-22 — Art direction owned here: severed stubs, the cap piece, and one shared carried-module sprite.** *Rule.* Boarding owns the **art direction and states** of the severed-stub pieces (kit gap K-9; DA:526, DA:1341, DA:1465) for `/asset-spec`: one STUB per open fluid port; hissing until capped (the sound is Audio's); a cap piece after `begin_cap`; the pedestal rect stays (LY-20) and the scorched kerf is Interaction's V8 completion row; all **hue-less, matte, mechanical, no glow, no body vocabulary in any sprite, palette or sound brief** (ART 4.4; the flesh and violet palette is reserved for sanity leaks). All carried modules, real or (VS-A) phantom, draw as **one plain shape drawn once on the tool layer** (ART:512, ART:815), so a real and a phantom carry look alike; identity is known from the loose sprite at pickup and from the body cue, not from the carried sprite (closes IA Open Question 23). The carry frames keep the chest point and the forward beam arc unobstructed (ART:1221); pose is **ruled by the art-director: the module is held overhead**, centred over the helmet with its bottom edge at least 2 px above the crown, the sprite capped at 24 x 14 px so figure plus module is at most 71 px, under the 72 px wall front face (ART 8.2; AD-BS-1, AD-BS-2; C-BS-5 closed). The PC-8 phrase "below the lamp" is refreshed (refresh row 19). *Why.* A hue-less, matte stub stays on the truth side of the sane palette, and one shared carried sprite means a real and a phantom carry can be told apart only by the body cue and the checks, never by the drawing. *Pillar.* P1, P4 (parity). *Decided.* run rulings R21, R22.

**BS-23 — Loose items and the sortie: what Boarding repeats.** *Rule.* Placement data is Derelict Authoring's ledger (DA-26), slots are Layout's (LY-22), the mechanism and holder are Interaction's (IA-28); Boarding adds only the sortie-level rules stated in BS-13 (abandon persists), BS-5 (dark drop needs light), BS-16 (the haul is finite). A module dropped then left on a section that is later welded or cut off moves with the section (items are section-local, LY-22). Wording refreshes owed to neighbours that say otherwise are listed in Cross-References and never edited here. *Why.* Restating loose-item ownership here would give one fact two writers; naming only the sortie-level consequences (abandon persists, a dark drop needs light, the haul is finite) keeps one owner per fact. *Pillar.* P5. *Decided.* run ruling R20.

**BS-24 — What Boarding refuses (the anti-pillar rule).** *Rule.* The following do not exist in the sortie, and a rule anywhere in this GDD that implies one is a defect:

| Refusal | Guard | Pillar or anti-pillar |
|---|---|---|
| Combat: no weapon on any def, no damage verb, no kill; the cutter cuts machines, never creatures | G-1 | not a combat game |
| Progression: nothing levels, unlocks or scores from salvage; the only progression is the ship's body and what the player learns (the knowledge persistence the failure contract depends on is not a progression this guard forbids) | G-2 | P5 / not a combat game |
| Construction: modules are pre-shaped; a take is an extraction and not a relocation; no `remove_module` | G-3 | not a freeform builder |
| Economy: no shop, currency, recipe, vendor, refill, loot table, rarity; salvage-once | G-4 | P5 |
| Readouts: no inventory screen, pack capacity, encumbrance meter, weight, value, condition or capacity number, pod scale | G-5 | P1 |
| Timers and meters: no sortie timer, no Attention meter (visual or audio), no `listen` verb | G-6 | P3 |
| Forced script: sortie order is a recommendation (DA-2); no forced drop, cap, required module or quest item; no payoff-less carry taught as the reference cut | G-7 | no authored story; P1 |
| PvP: no stealing from a crewmate's hands and no reward for harm (VS-B); the carry cost is the crew's shared cost; the only harm to a crewmate is the system-gated friendly-harm act, admitted only while the attacker's sanity is system-verified below the harm threshold (Sanity; IA-31), with a victim-visible tell and attribution to the web, so a sane player has no attack (P4(c)) | G-8 | no intentional PvP; P4(c) |
| Body metaphor in the senses: graft, severed, bleed are design prose only | G-9 | P1 sane palette |
| A win state in a haul: no "complete the ship", no collection checklist | G-10 | no win state |
| A price that lies (BS-15) | G-11 | P4 |

Restraint and retreat are legal strategies with stated costs (BS-13). *Pillar.* the anti-pillars. *Decided.* run ruling R31; creative-director guard rails G-1 to G-11.

### States and Transitions

**Boarding & Salvage has no stored state** (BS-1). The template requires a state table, so the table below is a **fixture and design vocabulary**: each label is a predicate over fields the owners already hold, computed and never written, never registered, never persisted, and never a name in any file, command or event.
