The neighbours that name Boarding in their own rows (IA:464, SE:471, IDB:469, MSA:502, DA:680, LY:443, AT:380, WM:414, SN:521, HE:376, BDY:341) receive the refresh table in Cross-References; none of them is edited here.

## Formulas

**Scope and conventions (read first; every formula below uses them).** Every formula here is **design-time: not hashed, not persisted and never read by the simulation** (Boarding registers no state and no constant, BS-1, R1; the SN:529 precedent). Each is an analysis that the designers and the headless reference sortie can recompute, and each leans on an owner's formula **by name and never restates it**: `attention_integrator` (Attention), `held_mass_cue` (Suit), IDB F-13 `haul_cost` and F-15 `patch_launder` (`T_cycle`), SN D.7 (check cost), DA D.10 `cold_room_exposure`, WM D.4 `hop_fuel`, SL `loss_ticks`. Arithmetic is integer: `ceil` and `floor` act on non-negative operands, shares are in permille, and every Attention result is the **discrete, run-quantised integer** of the integrator (the level `L` and baseline `B` in mAU, 1 AU = 1000 mAU = one walking tick of noise, six ticks per run), recomputed from `attention_integrator` with the light and power terms zeroed unless a row says otherwise; the closed-form `ln` and `e^` estimates of the specialist drafts are used only as labelled cross-checks. Three caveats apply to every number. (1) Every sortie time is a **lower bound** while the tool-swap duration is unregistered (`T_EQUIP`, SN OQ-SN-6, carried as 0), and `pickup` and `drop` are TAPs with no registered duration (carried as 0). (2) Every Attention result depends on the [PLACEHOLDER] values of `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD`, `TAU_ATT_TICKS`, `ATT_THRESHOLD`, `HOLD_PM` [PLACEHOLDER], `B_table`, `TELL_DRAW_LO` [PLACEHOLDER] and `TELL_DRAW_HI` [PLACEHOLDER]. (3) Every route figure rests on **assumed node anchors**: Derelict Authoring publishes room rects, door spans and the `arrive_*` cells but no node anchors, so legs are Manhattan distances from door centres to an anchor tagged `[ASSUMED anchor]`, and Layout's `route_length_px` governs once published (DA-29, ASK-BS-D1). *Orchestrator ruling: the specialist drafts disagreed on three inputs; this GDD adopts the checkpoint's `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 for the played tell (K1), Sanity's run of 12 ticks, 5 per second, for dwell (SN conventions; the primary draft assumed 10 per second), and the registry's per-step leak column of IDB-7 as already-halved (the primary draft applied `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` twice).*

| Formula | Serves | Reads (by name) | Answers |
|---|---|---|---|
| D.1 `route_ticks` | BS-2, BS-10, BS-11, BS-20 | IDB F-13, `V_WALK`, `V_RUN`, `V_CARRY`, `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] | how long a leg takes in each gait; the route floor of a sortie |
| D.2 `sortie_noise_budget` | BS-4, BS-7, BS-13 | `attention_integrator`, `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD`, `NOISE_PER_TICK` [PLACEHOLDER] | how much Attention a cut and a carry buy |
| D.3 `tell_window_fit` | BS-7, BS-13 | AT-8, AT-10, `abortability_play`, IA-16 | what a tell leaves for the cut, the carry, the run and the partial |
| D.4 `hazard_dwell_budget` | BS-17, BS-20 | DA D.10, `RESERVE_S` [PLACEHOLDER], DA-28 | how much of the cold clock or the reserve a take spends |
| D.5 `cap_versus_leave` | BS-8 | IDB-7, IDB F-12, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`, `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` | what a stub costs if capped and if left |
| D.6 `modules_per_fuel` | BS-12 | WM-20, WM D.4, WM D.5 | how many hauls the home tank allows |
| D.7 `check_cost_ratio` | BS-14 | SN D.7, SN-18, SN-21 | whether a SCAN is cheap enough against the trip home |
| D.8 `sortie_dwell_envelope` | BS-4, BS-20 | SN E1, `ABOARD_EXPOSURE`, `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` | how the sanity, fuel and cell clocks nest around a sortie |
| D.9 `loss_window_after_cut` | BS-21 | SL-4, SL-5, SL-8, `loss_ticks` | how long a finished cut can be lost to a death |
| D.10 `lamp_cell_budget` | BS-5, BS-19 | `CELL_S` [PLACEHOLDER], SE lamp rules | how many sorties a full cell lights |

### D.1 `route_ticks`

The route_ticks formula is defined as:

`route_ticks(px, mode, n_closed) = ceil(px / v[mode]) + n_closed * DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` ; `route_noise_AU(px, mode) = ceil(px / v[mode]) * NOISE_PER_TICK[mode]` (IDB F-13, cited) ; `T_sortie_floor = 2 * (HOP_LEG_TICKS + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS) + route_ticks(px_in, walk, n_in) + SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS + route_ticks(px_out, carry, 0) + route_ticks(px_home, carry, n_home)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `px` | int px | 0 to about 2880 (F-13: 1 to 60 tiles) | Layout `route_length_px` governs; MVP legs are Manhattan distances over DA rects and door spans to an `[ASSUMED anchor]` | the length of one leg |
| `mode` | enum | walk, carry, run | Controller PC-8 | the gait of the leg |
| `v[mode]` | int px/tick | `V_WALK` 2, `V_CARRY` 1, `V_RUN` 3 | registry (Controller, locked) | speed per gait; carry is not scaled by mass (IDB-21) |
| `n_closed` | int | 0 to 3 | DA door tables | doors on the leg that start CLOSED and have not been opened; one drop-open-pickup cycle each while carrying (BS-10) |
| `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 (18 to 60) [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction IA-24 | a bare-hand V4 attempt, QUIET |
| `NOISE_PER_TICK[mode]` | int AU/tick | walk 1, run 4, carry 1 [PLACEHOLDER] | Controller PC-10 | Attention units per tick |
| `HOP_LEG_TICKS`, `ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` | int ticks | 900 (360 to 2190), 45 (24 to 180), both [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map WM-34, WM-14 | one hop out and one hop home are each leg plus settle |
| `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 240 (180 to 480) [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction IA-25 | the cut |

**Output Range:** ticks from 0 to a few thousand per leg; `T_sortie_floor` is a **lower bound** (the two TAPs, `T_EQUIP` and the player's reading time are 0).

**Example:** the legs (walk ticks are `ceil(px / 2)`, carry ticks equal `px`, run ticks are `ceil(px / 3)`):

| Leg | px | tiles | walk | carry | run |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `arrive_a_dock` to `htr_a` via `door_a_dock_spine` and `door_a_spine_machine` (both start CLOSED), `[ASSUMED anchor (600, 480)]` | 624 | 13.0 | 312 | 624 | 208 |
| `arrive_a_dock` to `scr_a`, `[ASSUMED anchor (576, 216)]` | 576 | 12.0 | 288 | 576 | 192 |
| `arrive_b_dock` to `scr_b`, two doors, `[ASSUMED anchor (624, 312)]` | 504 | 10.5 | 252 | 504 | 168 |
| `arrive_home_airlock` to `hp_cargo_heater`, `door_cargo_s` (doorway x [528, 624), DA C.1) starts CLOSED, `[ASSUMED anchor (552, 240)]` | 456 | 9.5 | 228 | 456 | 152 |

The reference sortie (line D, `htr_a`): walk in `312 + 2 * 30` = 372 ticks (the player opens both doors on the way in and they stay open: *open on the way in, carry through on the way out*); cut 240; carry out 624; hop 945 each way; at home 456 plus one closed door (`door_cargo_s`) at 30 = 486 (re-derived 2026-10-27 after Derelict Authoring moved `door_cargo_s` from `doorway_start_px` 552 to 528: the leg is the straight column x 552 from the arrival cell (552, 696), which lies inside the new 96 px doorway [528, 624), so the Manhattan length stays 456 and neither 486 nor `T_sortie_floor` changes). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Door-state preconditions of the reference sortie (stated, not assumed):** at A, `door_a_dock_spine` and `door_a_spine_machine` start CLOSED and the player opens both on the way in and leaves them open; at home, `door_airlock_n` (initial CLOSED, DA C.1) lies on the only route from the cryo bay to the pod, so the player has opened it on the way out and it stays OPEN, while `door_cargo_s` (initial CLOSED) is not on that route and starts CLOSED. That gives `n_home` = 1 and the home leg 456 + 30 = 486. If `door_airlock_n` were found closed (a door shut behind the player, EC-BS-59) `n_home` = 2, the leg is 516 and `T_sortie_floor` 3642; if both were open the leg is 456 and `T_sortie_floor` 3582. The 486 and 3612 figures are therefore the figures under these stated preconditions, and AC-BS-6 fixes them as its starting state. `T_sortie_floor = 2 * 945 + 372 + 240 + 624 + 486` = **3612 ticks = 60.2 s**. A survey allowance (a player's choices, not rules: listen 600, three rooms read for 360 ticks each = 1080, one SCAN of `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 [PLACEHOLDER]) lifts it to **5412 ticks = 90.2 s**. The design target of about five minutes (18,000 ticks, BS-20) is therefore **player time** (standing still, reading, deciding, door choices, the pump-stall clock if the bus is woken), not distance; nobody tunes the route up to reach it, because `V_CARRY` is locked. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **One window per metric.** *W_LOOP* runs from the home console press to the install: its floor is `T_sortie_floor` 3612 ticks (60.2 s) and its survey-allowance figure 5412 ticks (90.2 s); the pod legs (1890 ticks, 31.5 s) are 52 percent of the floor and 35 percent of the survey figure. *W_ABOARD* runs from `pod_settle` at the derelict to the departing console press and is the window of Sanity's dwell (D.8), of the lit time (D.10) and of X-BS-1: its floor is 372 + 240 + 624 = 1236 ticks (20.6 s) and the survey allowance adds 600 + 1080 + 120 = 1800 ticks (30 s) for 3036 ticks (50.6 s). The concept's five-minute loop is a W_LOOP figure; at about 41 s of pod legs, home carry and install it leaves about 259 s of W_ABOARD, which is what nests inside the stage-1 point of D.8. **Nothing in the content produces that time:** the aboard floor is 20.6 s and the survey allowance 50.6 s, so about 200 s of the target is discretionary dwell the design does not yet budget, and the pod transit share is a pacing lever (`HOP_LEG_TICKS` is a pacing knob, Tuning Knobs, not only a fuel and fiction constant). Re-baselining the target is a user decision (OQ-BS-28). Noise of the same legs (F-13): carrying `htr_a` out costs `624 * 1` = 624 AU raw; dropping it and running costs `208 * 4` = 832 AU raw, louder in total but a third of the time (D.3).

### D.2 `sortie_noise_budget`

The sortie_noise_budget formula is defined as:

`L' = clamp(L + I_run - ceil((L - B) * 6 / TAU_ATT_TICKS), B, L_CEIL)` per 6-tick run, which is Attention's `attention_integrator` (F-5) with the **full** intake `I_run = I_noise + I_light + I_power` (stage-12 restatement: the sortie pushes all three at once, and the earlier text that set `I_light = I_power = 0` described the lower bound only). `I_noise = 1000 * (sum over the run's ticks of NOISE_PER_TICK[mode]) + 1000 * U_TOOL * (accrued tool-channel ticks in the run) + 1000 * U_LOUD * [the run holds the cut's completion tick]` mAU ; `I_light` (Attention F-3) is the owner's chest beam counted once at apex value, `W_L[BEAM] * floor(output_pm * reach_px / 1000) * 6 div 1000` = 1000 x 288 x 6 div 1000 = **1,728 mAU per run (288 mAU per tick)** while the beam is on, plus every lit fixture of every loaded section ; `I_power` (Attention F-4) is `(W_P * nl_w * 6) div 1000 + (W_S * max(0, nl_w - nl_w_five_runs_ago) * 6) div 1000`, with `nl_w = max(0, draw_w - lamps_lit_w)`, of the lit home ship while it is loaded *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* ; `share_pm(L) = floor(1000 * L / (ATT_THRESHOLD * 1000))`. **The sortie's push is `I_run`; the noise-only trace is its lower bound.**

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L`, `B` | int mAU | `B` 300,000 (ring 1) to `L_CEIL` 6,120,000 | Attention `attention_integrator`; `B_table` [PLACEHOLDER] | the level and the ring baseline |
| `I_run` | int mAU per run | 0 to 204,000 (a cut run of 6 accrued ticks plus the one-shot) | Interaction IA-33 and Controller PC-10 supply the pushes | the noise intake of one run |
| `NOISE_PER_TICK[mode]` | int AU/tick | walk 1, run 4, carry 1, idle 0 [PLACEHOLDER] | Controller | locomotion noise |
| `U_TOOL` | int AU/tick | 4 (2 to 6) [PLACEHOLDER] | Attention | one accrued cut or cap tick |
| `U_LOUD` | int AU | 180 (30 x `U_TOOL` to 400) [PLACEHOLDER] | Attention | the one-shot at cut completion |
| `TAU_ATT_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 (1200 to 1816) [PLACEHOLDER] | Attention (registry: `TAU_ATT_TICKS`; 1817 fails F-15, 3600 is only the arithmetic ceiling of F-5) | the memory constant |
| `ATT_THRESHOLD`, `HOLD_PM` | int AU, permille | 6000, 800 (`theta_HOLD` 4800 AU) [PLACEHOLDER] | Attention | tell entry and the abort line |
| `L_CEIL` | int mAU | `ATT_THRESHOLD * L_CEIL_PM / 1000` = 6,120,000 (`L_CEIL_PM` 1020 [PLACEHOLDER]) | Attention AT-5 | the saturation of the level |
| `I_light`, `I_power` | int mAU per run | beam 288 mAU per tick; the lit idle home ship 766 mAU per tick (576 light from three fixtures and the EMERGENCY lamp, 190 power from 1,900 W at `W_P` 100) | Attention F-3, F-4 and the F-25 row "Home ship lit and idle" | **part of the push** in every row below labelled "beam and home" (the lit rows); the "noise only" rows omit them and are a lower bound, never the whole push (C-BS-13) |

**Output Range:** `B <= L' <= L_CEIL`; the useful read is the peak as a share of `ATT_THRESHOLD`. Never shown to the player (AT-20).

**Example (ring 1, integer recursion, recomputed; the route is D.1's `[ASSUMED anchor]` route). Read the lit figure first: the player pushes noise, light and power at once. The lit reference sortie (beam on throughout, home ship lit and idle, `L0` 1678.5 AU) peaks at 3394.8 AU, 57 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD`, at the end of the carry. The 29 percent figure below is the noise term alone (`I_light = I_power = 0`, `L0 = B`): a lower bound that omits two of the three terms and must not be read as the push.** The reference sortie pushes walk-in 312 ticks at 1, two door attempts (QUIET, 60 idle ticks), the cut 240 ticks at `U_TOOL` plus `U_LOUD`, the pickup, the carry out 624 ticks at 1, the console. Raw pushed: `312 + (240 * 4 + 180) + 624` = **2076 AU**, of which the cut is **1140 AU** (19 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD`). From `L = B`: peak **1756.6 AU** (29 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD`, 37 percent of `theta_HOLD`) at the end of the carry, then idle decay. Rows (the **golden-fixture schedule**, in order and in ticks: walk-in 312 at 1 AU, door attempts 60 idle, the cut 240 at `U_TOOL` plus `U_LOUD` on the last tick, the pickup TAP 12 idle (a fixture dwell, not an owner's duration), the carry 624 at 1 AU, the console approach 30 idle; exact results 1,756,600 mAU peak and 1,732,482 mAU after the console from `L0 = B`; 3,394,795 and 3,374,965 mAU with the beam and home terms from 1,678,501 mAU. Omitting the 12-tick pickup dwell moves the noise-only peak to 1,762,817 mAU, so the figures are exact only for this schedule):

| Scenario | Peak `L` | Share of `ATT_THRESHOLD` | Share of `theta_HOLD` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Reference sortie, lit: noise plus the beam (288) plus the lit idle home ship (766 mAU/tick), from `L0` 1678.5 AU (home idle steady state)** | **3394.8 AU** | **57 percent** | **71 percent** |
| Reference sortie, noise term only, `L0 = B` 300 AU (lower bound; omits `I_light` and `I_power`) | 1756.6 AU | 29 percent | 37 percent |
| Three cuts in one room with 3-tile walks between, with beam and home terms | 4741.7 AU | 79 percent | 99 percent |
| Same, noise term only (lower bound) | 3165.2 AU | 53 percent | 66 percent |

Cuts to trip: chaining cut-and-walk cycles (240 ticks, one LOUD, 72 walking ticks), the tenth cycle's report crosses `ATT_THRESHOLD` on noise alone (3120 ticks, 52 s); with the beam and the home term the **fifth** does (1560 ticks, 26 s). Continuous running from rest reaches it in 2820 ticks (47.0 s) on noise alone and in 1476 ticks (24.6 s) with beam and home terms, which reproduces the Attention GDD's own scenario row (F-25: "crosses `theta` from idle in 1,476 ticks (24.6 s)"). *Finding (RK-A, C-BS-13; revised at stage 12 after the CD-GDD-ALIGN concern):* at the placeholders the lit reference sortie peaks at 57 percent of `ATT_THRESHOLD` and opens no tell, and a chain of cuts with the beam on does within half a minute, so "rising Attention" at the MVP comes from the lit job, running and chained cuts and not from a single cut. **A sweep of the three intake constants this formula reads over the lit golden schedule (`U_TOOL` 2 to 6, `U_LOUD` 30 x `U_TOOL` to 400, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1200 to 1816, the registry's safe range, 1817 failing Attention's abortability F-15; same integer recursion, run-quantised) never reaches `ATT_THRESHOLD`: the extreme corner (`U_TOOL` 6, `U_LOUD` 400, `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1816) peaks at 3884 AU (65 percent), and at `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 1800 the best corner peaks at 3865 AU (64 percent). A lit listen of 300 or 600 ticks before the walk-in adds only 40 or 74 AU to the peak.** *(Re-swept 2026-10-27, consistency pass: the earlier sweep ran to `TAU_ATT_TICKS` 3600, outside the registry's safe range.)* `[UNV: the sweep is this GDD's own recomputation, regenerated by the headless fixture; Attention to confirm]`. So no retune of `U_TOOL`, `U_LOUD` or `TAU_ATT_TICKS` alone makes the reference sortie meet a tell; Attention's other levers (`NOISE_PER_TICK`, `W_L`, `W_P`, `B_table`, `ATT_THRESHOLD`) must move with them, or the route and node density (Derelict Authoring) must grow. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the choice is now a three-owner user decision, OQ-BS-27, set out in the paragraphs below.)* The choice of lever was Attention's (ASK-BS-A1, widened to these constants and made a precondition of the MVP playtest); the **outcome it must achieve is Boarding's design target X-BS-11** (Game Feel), so the 29 percent figure can no longer be read as the whole push and the MVP playtest cannot test a sortie that has no breach rhythm. The envelope rows assume the lit home ship's term keeps feeding the player's level while the crew is aboard, as the F-25 rows do `[UNV: Attention to confirm]`. The closed-form cross-check (`E(t+dt) = E(t) e^(-dt/tau) + r tau (1 - e^(-dt/tau))`) gives about 1467 AU of excess at the end of the carry against the recursion's 1456.6 AU (1756.6 less `B`), inside the quantisation.

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **What D.2 can and cannot settle.** The sweep above shows that the intake constants alone never cross; the design review adds that crossing is not sufficient. The reference decision needs three properties together: (i) *crossing*: the lit golden schedule's peak reaches `ATT_THRESHOLD` with margin; (ii) *causality*: the cut and the carry supply at least about 50 percent of the headroom above `L0`, so the tell is the player's doing (28 percent of the threshold is home idle and is not a cause); (iii) *a live choice*: `fits_carry(px, T)` is false for part of `TELL_DRAW` at the tick the tell opens, so walking on does not always win (D.3), and freezing is either priced or explicitly free. The rulings that deliver them are options A, B and C of **OQ-BS-27 (USER DECISION)**; none is a retune and none is chosen here. *Option sizing, for the ruling (design-time, `[UNV: Attention and Derelict Authoring to confirm]`).* **(A) Move the decision:** the tell opens at or just after the cut's LOUD report, so the decision becomes "cut into a rising tell or not"; no new intake is needed, the carry then fits by 276 to 1,176 ticks (`T` 900 to 1800 less 624), and the anchor and X-BS-4 are rewritten to match. **(B) Add a carry term:** Attention adds a carry or sortie-burden intake term of about 7,000 mAU per tick while carrying (not a mass term, so IDB-21 holds) and the decision-bearing carry is lengthened to about 21 to 28 tiles (DA-28 allows 28) so that `fits_carry` is false for the lower part of `TELL_DRAW`. **(C) Lengthen the reference decision:** the reference decision sortie is a longer B route or a second A node. **Freeze** (stand still after the cut so the tell lapses, D.3) is named as an explicit option in every branch: priced (for example by the stillness-abort timing or a dwell cost) or intentionally free, and never left implicit.

**Acceptance shape (option-independent).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* One generated golden file, **`REF_GOLDEN`**, is produced by running the headless reference schedule against Attention's real integrator at the live constants. It replaces every hard-coded placeholder figure in the acceptance rows (AC-BS-6(e), 14a, 14b, 15, 16, 56, 122). It records, for the lit schedule and for three variants (beam off; home lamps off; short dwell, meaning no listen and no pickup dwell), the per-run `L`, the peak, `tell_open_tick` (or none) and the `phase` at the console press. The assertions are deterministic margins on that one file: (a) the lit run crosses, with `peak >= 1.05 x ATT_THRESHOLD`, and `tell_open_tick` lies in the window the ruled option names; (b) each of the three variants stays at or below `0.95 x theta_HOLD`, so restraint is a real strategy and the variants are not knife-edge; (c) causality: with the cut's and the carry's pushes replaced by idle, the lit peak falls by at least 50 percent of the headroom `ATT_THRESHOLD - L0`; (d) the decision is live: `fits_carry` is false for some tell draw in [`TELL_DRAW_LO`, `TELL_DRAW_HI`] at the tick the tell opens (options B and C), or the tell opens at or after the cut's LOUD report (option A). Independent of the file, three **oracle cases** are computed by hand and are never read from it: **O-1**, one run holding the cut-completion tick from `L = B` 300,000 mAU: `I_run` = 1000 x 4 x 6 + 1000 x 180 = 204,000 mAU, so `L'` = 504,000 mAU; **O-2**, one idle run from there: `ceil(204,000 x 6 / 1800)` = 680, so `L'` = 503,320 mAU; **O-3**, `L = B` with no intake leaves `L = B`. Until the ruling, the file records the placeholder snapshot printed in this section and assertion (a) is expected to fail: that failure is the finding, and it is not waived.

### D.3 `tell_window_fit`

The tell_window_fit formula is defined as:

`carry_tiles(T) = floor(T * V_CARRY / TILE_PX)` ; `run_tiles(T) = floor(T * V_RUN / TILE_PX)` ; `T_after_cut = T - SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` ; `fits_carry(px, T) <=> px <= T * V_CARRY` ; `t_abort(L_cancel) = t_decay(L_cancel) + ABORT_HOLD_TICKS - GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (the first run under the line counts as run 1, AT-10, so the abort fires on the 20th run under `theta_HOLD`) ; `partial_alive <=> t_abort <= PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` (inclusive, IA-16)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int ticks | `TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 to `TELL_DRAW_HI` 1800 played [PLACEHOLDER]; `TELL_MIN_TICKS` 600 (derived) only via `attention_arm_tell` and fixtures | Attention AT-8, AT-21 | the tell time remaining when the carry (or the cut) begins; a tell at entry has `tell_len` in [900, 1800] |
| `px` | int px | D.1 legs | Layout / DA `[ASSUMED anchor]` | the carry route to the console |
| `V_CARRY`, `V_RUN`, `TILE_PX` | int | 1, 3, 48 | registry (locked) | gait speeds and tile size |
| `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 240 [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction | a cut begun at tell entry leaves `T - 240` |
| `t_decay(L_cancel)` | int ticks | 480 from `theta`, 516 from `L_CEIL` | recomputed from `attention_integrator` with the dark floor 294 mAU/tick (AT F-16); Attention's `abortability_play` gives 540 to `theta_HOLD - ATT_MARGIN` from `L_CEIL` at ring 1 | the stillness-and-dark time to fall under `theta_HOLD` |
| `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (60 to 300) [PLACEHOLDER] | Attention AT-10 | consecutive time under `theta_HOLD` that aborts the tell |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | 600 (180 to 1200) [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction IA-16 | how long a cancelled cut keeps its progress |

**Output Range:** booleans and tile counts per `T`; `carry_tiles` 12 to 37, `run_tiles` 37 to 112 over `T` 600 to 1800.

**Example:**

| `T` (tell ticks remaining) | carry tiles | run tiles | `T_after_cut` | carry tiles after a cut | run tiles after a cut |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 600 (fixture floor, `TELL_MIN_TICKS`) | 12 | 37 | 360 | 7 | 22 |
| 900 (shortest played, `TELL_DRAW_LO`) | 18 | 56 | 660 | 13 | 41 |
| 1200 | 25 | 75 | 960 | 20 | 60 |
| 1800 (longest, `TELL_DRAW_HI`) | 37 | 112 | 1560 | 32 | 97 |

`htr_a` (13.0 tiles, 624 ticks, `[ASSUMED anchor]`): a tell that opens as the cut begins, at the shortest played draw, leaves 660 ticks after the cut, so the carry fits with 36 ticks to spare; at the 600-tick fixture floor it leaves 360 and does not fit, while dropping the module and running (208 ticks) always fits. `scr_a` (12.0 tiles, 576 ticks) fits by 84. The fit means *reach the console before the tell expires*; the hop stays legal inside a BREACH (WM-26), so a late arrival is a hazard and not a refusal. **Stillness and the partial:** a player who cancels the cut at `theta` and goes dark and still falls under `theta_HOLD` after 480 ticks, and the tell aborts after `480 + 120 - 6` = **594 ticks**, six ticks inside the inclusive `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; from `L_CEIL` it is `516 + 120 - 6` = **630 ticks**, thirty past it (Attention's F-15b adds the full `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` and gets 660 from `L_CEIL` with its margin). So a cancelled cut's partial survives an abort-by-stillness only at the lowest entry levels and only if the cut is resumed within six ticks of the abort; otherwise the cut restarts from zero. That is Attention's "restraint usually costs the partial" (AT-10) arrived at through the ledger rather than the abort. A cut in progress is loud and keeps `L` above `theta_HOLD`, so the abort is forfeited for as long as the cut runs (AT-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Liveness and the price of freezing.** The fit table above is the decision as it stands, and it is dead: `htr_a` fits at every played draw, with 36 ticks to spare when the tell opens as the cut begins and 276 to 1,176 ticks when it opens at the cut's LOUD report (`T` 900 to 1800 less 624), so walking on always wins. `fits_carry` is false for part of the draw only if the carry is longer than the draw: at a tell opened at the report, a 21-tile carry (1008 px) fails for `T` 900 to 1007 and a 28-tile carry (1344 px, DA-28's bound) fails for `T` 900 to 1343, about half the draws; that is the sizing behind option B of OQ-BS-27. Freezing is the third option: after the cut the module is on the deck, and standing still and dark lets the tell lapse after 594 to 630 ticks at the cost of the dwell, `about 50 runs * 19 mSU` = 940 mSU = **0.94 percent of `SAN_MAX`** (D.8), and the module is still there. It is cheap enough to be a free option, and whether to price it or leave it free is part of the ruling (OQ-BS-27). *Not hashed, not persisted.*

### D.4 `hazard_dwell_budget`

The hazard_dwell_budget formula is defined as:

`dwell = ceil(px_in / V_WALK) + T_open + n_cuts * SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS + T_scan + px_out / V_CARRY` ; `margin_pm = floor(1000 * dwell / T_crit)` ; for the unbreathable branch `reserve_pm = floor(1000 * round_trip_ticks / (RESERVE_S * TICK_RATE))` ; `T_crit` is DA D.10's `runs_critical * GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (cited, not re-derived)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `px_in`, `px_out` | int px | up to 7 tiles x 48 = 336 inside the cold room | DA-28 (the crossing bound); `[ASSUMED anchor]` 204 px | the in-room legs, walked in and carried out |
| `T_open` | int ticks | 0 or 30 | Interaction IA-24 (`DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]) | opening `door_a_spine_machine` from outside costs no cold; count 0 if the sill is not crossed |
| `n_cuts` | int | 1 to 8 | the player | cuts made in one visit |
| `T_scan` | int ticks | 0 or 120 | Interaction (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER]) | a SCAN taken inside |
| `T_crit` | int ticks | 2184 (364 runs, CRITICAL at `T0` -22000 mC); FAILED at 6546 (1091 runs) | DA D.10 / Suit D.4 | the cold clock at the room's authored temperature |
| `round_trip_ticks` | int ticks | up to 2688 (56 tiles at `V_CARRY`) | DA-28 bound | a carried round trip through an unbreathable stretch |
| `RESERVE_S` | int s | 90 (45 to 240) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | the engaged reserve breath |

**Output Range:** `dwell` from `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` (240, a cut with no crossing) to `dwell_fixed + n_cuts * SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` (2226 for eight cuts at the defaults, where `dwell_fixed` = 306 = 102 + 0 + 204 without a SCAN); `margin_pm` above 1000 means CRITICAL is reached. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `reserve_pm` is undefined for `RESERVE_S` 0 (the Suit range floor is 45, so it cannot occur; a `RESERVE_S` of 0 is a configuration error, not a sentinel) and `T_crit` must be positive (DA D.10 guarantees it). A route with `margin_pm >= 1000` for its authored crossing is a Derelict Authoring lint failure, not a Boarding rule. The Graph's room cools and the gain rises over a long visit, so these margins shrink; they are exact only at `T0`.

**Example:** `htr_a` with the assumed anchors: `102 + 0 + 240 + 0 + 204` = **546 ticks = 9.1 s = 250 permille** of `T_crit` 2184. With one SCAN: 666 (30 percent). Worst case inside DA's 7-tile bound with a SCAN: `168 + 0 + 240 + 120 + 336` = **864 ticks = 14.4 s = 395 permille** (`floor(1000 * 864 / 2184)`), leaving 1320 ticks (22 s). Each further cut in the visit adds 240: `dwell(n) = 306 + 240 n` gives 1026 (47 percent) for three, 1506 (69 percent) for five, 1986 (91 percent) for seven, and **2226 for eight, which exceeds 2184**: `a_machine` holds about eight module nodes (DA C.2), so stripping the room is lethal by construction; it tolerates one purposeful cut and a shuttle, not a strip. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *("eight cuts" holds only at the default `T_crit` 2184 and `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240. As a function of the live constants the lethal count is `n_lethal = floor((T_crit - dwell_fixed) / SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS) + 1`, which is 8 at the defaults (`floor((2184 - 306) / 240) + 1`) and moves with `A_MACHINE_T_MC`, `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` and the anchors. The statement "stripping `a_machine` is lethal" is therefore a lint on Derelict Authoring's side, `module_nodes(a_machine) >= n_lethal`, and not a constant of this GDD.)* The irony is the authored one (DA-28): `htr_a` is the heater that keeps `a_machine` survivable once the bus is woken, so cutting it removes the thing that would have made lingering safe. Unbreathable branch (B's `b_breach`): a 56-tile carried round trip is 2688 ticks = 44.8 s = **497 permille** of `RESERVE_S` 90 s, and a drop-and-return adds a second traverse (EC-BS-38). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Reserve constraint (a lint owed to Derelict Authoring and Suit, not a Boarding constant):** at `RESERVE_S` 45 (the floor) the reserve is 2700 ticks against DA's 2688-tick bound, a margin of 12 ticks, which one door cycle (30) or one pickup dwell (12) exceeds; a minimal incident therefore needs `RESERVE_S * TICK_RATE >= 2688 + 42` = 2730, that is `RESERVE_S >= 46`, and the DA-28 carry bound combined with the range floor is lethal below that. A deliberate drop-and-return (two traverses, 5376 ticks = 89.6 s) leaves only 24 ticks at the default `RESERVE_S` 90. **Open-door cost of the carry out:** leaving `door_a_spine_machine` open on the way in lets `K_OPEN` conduction cool `a_spine`, and the carry back through it is about 288 of the reference 624 ticks (`[ASSUMED anchor]`); that cost is Derelict Authoring's open-door projection printed beside every closed-door row by `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` (DA D.18, AC-DA-40) and is cited here, not recomputed. *Design-time; DA's lints are the authority.*

### D.5 `cap_versus_leave`

The cap_versus_leave formula is defined as:

`leak_step(p) = floor(cap_p * OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000)` ; `steps = floor(t_s * TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS)` ; `leak_total = (sum over open fluid ports of leak_step(p)) * steps` ; `share_pm = min(1000, floor(1000 * leak_total / n_room))` for `n_room >= 1`, and the sentinel `NO_GAS` (nothing left to lose, `leak_total` effectively 0) for `n_room = 0` (a vented room, `P_dkPa` 0), with `n_room = V * GAS_SCALE * P_dkPa` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* ; `cap_cost_ticks = n_stubs * CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS + T_EQUIP` ; `cap_cost_AU = n_stubs * CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS * U_TOOL`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cap_p` | int mGU/step (loop 1) or mL/step (loops 3 and 4) | scrubber 101: 400; O2 tank 103: 1200; vent 104: 600; pump 304: 40 (two); reservoir 303: 40; reactor 402: 2; fuel tank 403: 5; power-only modules: no fluid port | Item DB IDB-7 declared caps | the port's rated flow |
| `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` | int permille | 500 (100 to 1000) | Graph (registry: `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`; LSG:960) | the share of the cap that leaks per step until `cap_port` |
| `leak_step(p)` | int per step | scrubber 200; O2 tank 600; vent 300; pump 20 each; reservoir 20; reactor 1; fuel tank 2 | IDB-7 severed-leak column = IDB F-12's `floor(cap * OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000)` | already-halved: the table value **is** the leak |
| `t_s` | int s | 0 to about 480 | the session (WM `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 ticks, 8 min) | time spent aboard with the stub open |
| `V`, `P_dkPa` | int tiles, int dkPa | `a_life` 30; A 118 in total; standard 1000 | DA C.2 and the Graph (`STD_PRESSURE_DKPA` 1000) | the room volume and its pressure |
| `GAS_SCALE` | int | 1000 mGU per GU | Graph (registry) | unit scale |
| `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 90 (60 to 180) | Interaction, owner's constant, not a standalone registry entry | one cap |
| `T_EQUIP` | int ticks | unregistered, 0 here | SN OQ-SN-6 | the welder swap |

**Output Range:** `leak_total >= 0` and 0 for power-only modules; `share_pm` 0 to 1000 (clamped), and over a full safe sortie (`t_s` 480, 4800 steps) **32 permille for the scrubber stub on `a_life` (30 tiles)**, 48 for a vent stub and 96 for an O2-tank stub in a 30-tile room, scaling as 30 / `V` in a smaller room *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the earlier "0 to about 20" held only to `t_s` 300); `cap_cost` 90 ticks (1.5 s) and 360 AU raw per stub, plus the swap. The decision is the boolean *cap* and is the player's; the formula only prints the prices.

**Example:** `scr_a`'s stub on `a_life` (`n_room` = 30 * 1000 * 1000 = 30,000,000 mGU at standard pressure, `[UNV: DA authors A's rooms breathable]`): 200 mGU per step, 10 steps per second. A 60 s linger loses `200 * 600` = 120,000 mGU = **4 permille (0.4 percent)**; 150 s loses 1.0 percent; 300 s loses 2.0 percent. Against `R_BREATH` [PLACEHOLDER] 300 mGU per step per crew (Suit-owned, cited by the Graph) the stub is two-thirds of one person's breath. Capping costs 90 ticks and 360 AU. **Finding (RK-7, recorded and not repaired):** on a derelict you leave, the leave cost within one sortie is negligible, and a departed derelict goes dormant so the leak stops (LS-17, WM-24); capping pays for the **weld donor** only, because a welded section's `open_end` ports would keep leaking into the home body: an uncapped 200 mGU scrubber stub against the home 3,000,000 mGU O2 tank (IDB F-14) loses `200 * 600` = 120,000 mGU = **4 percent per minute**, and an O2-tank stub (600) 12 percent per minute (answered by the Graph, LSG:794: a welded section's stub leaks from the home network's pool once the seam valve is opened, and from the section's own stock while it is closed; the two figures assume the valve open) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. No pressure is invented to make capping matter (BS-8).

### D.6 `modules_per_fuel`

The modules_per_fuel formula is defined as:

`hop_ok(pool) <=> pool - HOP_FUEL_ML >= HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` (a hop from HOME; WM D.4) ; `hops_max(t) = max(0, floor((F0 - burn(t) - HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML) / HOP_FUEL_ML))` ; `burn(t) = floor(P_W * FUEL_UML_PER_W * 600 * t_min / 1,000,000)` mL ; `modules_max = hops_max` at one carried module per seat per hop (BS-12)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F0` | int mL | 20,000 at wake (`ft_main`) | WM D.4 (the Graph's FUEL_TANK `stock_ml`) | the home pool; no MVP refuel source (WM-20) |
| `HOP_FUEL_ML` | int mL | 400 (100 to 2000) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map WM-20 | debited on departure from HOME only; the return is prepaid (`RETURN_HOP_FUEL_ML` kept 0) |
| `HOP_FUEL_FLOOR_ML` | int mL | 1500 (1440 to 4000) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map WM D.5 | the pool that must remain after the debit |
| `P_W` | int W | 0 to 2500 | the Graph (home draw) | the reactor's load |
| `FUEL_UML_PER_W` | int | 120 (40 to 400) | Graph Tuning Knobs, not a registry entry (LSG:958) | burn per watt per step |
| `t_min` | int min | 0 to about 100 | the session | elapsed campaign time |

**Output Range:** `hops_max` 0 to 46 (no burn); a `berth` is a separate `BERTH_FUEL_ML` 800 [PLACEHOLDER] debit (WM-21).

**Example:** no burn: `floor((20000 - 1500) / 400)` = **46**; the 46th hop is accepted at pool 2000 (`2000 - 400 >= 1500`), the 47th refused `FUEL_LOW` at 1600. With the reactor at full draw (2500 W, 180 mL per minute, WM D.5): after 30 min `floor((20000 - 5400 - 1500) / 400)` = 32; after 60 min 19; after 90 min 5; after 100 min 1; the last hop is accepted at a burn of 18,100 mL, about 100.56 min. One hop debits 400 mL = 2 percent of the tank = 2.2 minutes of full-draw burn. **Finding (retracts the part-1 claim, C-BS-14):** fuel is a campaign clock shared with the reactor and bounds the late campaign; it does not bound a sortie, and it is not the cap on a haul. Three useful modules from A cost three hops, 1200 mL, 6 percent of the tank. The pressure on a haul is the one carried instance, the slow walk and the tell. The MVP end-to-end script (home to A, home to B, berth B, weld, A again) debits 400, 0, 400, 0, 800, 400 = 2000 mL, 10 percent of the tank (WM D.4). *All values [PLACEHOLDER] (Web Map); revisit at the kill/continue playtest.*

### D.7 `check_cost_ratio`

The check_cost_ratio formula is defined as:

`C_check = ceil(REACH_PX / V_WALK) + T_EQUIP + FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` ticks (SN D.7, cited) ; `check_ok <=> 20 * C_check <= T_home` (equivalently `C_check <= 0.05 * T_home`, SN-21, cross-multiplied so it stays integer) ; `T_home(a) = carry_ticks(px_to_console)` ; `T_home(b) = T_home(a) + HOP_LEG_TICKS + ARRIVE_SETTLE_TICKS` ; `T_home(c) = T_home(b) + route_ticks(px_home, carry, n_home)` ; `d* = (FIELD_CHECK_TICKS / TICK_RATE) * (V_WALK * TICK_RATE / TILE_PX)` tiles (SN-18, cited)

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `C_check` (SCAN) | int ticks | 156 (2.6 s): 36 reach + 0 swap + 120 | Sanity SN D.7 and AC-SN-70 | the cost of a SCAN, a lower bound |
| `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | int ticks | 120 (90 to 240) [PLACEHOLDER] | Interaction IA-30 | the lit channel | *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*
| `T_home` | int ticks | readings (a), (b), (c) | Sanity: "the one-way trip home with the item" (SN D.7); the endpoint is unstated | the trip the check is weighed against |
| `px_to_console`, `px_home` | int px | D.1 legs | `[ASSUMED anchor]` | the carry in the derelict and at home |
| `d*` | int tiles | 5 at `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` 120 | Sanity SN-18, AC-SN-70 | a witness nearer than 5 tiles is cheaper to look at than to scan |

**Output Range:** `ratio = C_check / T_home` from about 0.05 (pass) to 0.25; pass is `ratio <= 0.05`, that is `T_home >= 3120` ticks (52 s) for a SCAN.

**Example:** `htr_a`'s route (624 ticks): reading (a) 624 ticks, ratio **0.250**, fails; (b) with the 945-tick hop, 1569, **0.099**, fails; (c) with the 486-tick home carry, 2055, **0.076**, fails. DA's 28-tile one-way carry bound (1344 ticks): (a) 0.116, (b) 0.068, (c) 2775, **0.056**, fails narrowly. **Finding (C-BS-11):** a SCAN passes Sanity's 5 percent test on no MVP route under any reading; for reading (c) the carry alone would have to be at least `3120 - 945 - 486` = 1689 ticks (35 tiles), for reading (a) 3120 ticks (65 tiles, past DA's 56-tile unbreathable round-trip bound). It is not a defect at the MVP, because class (d) ships no junk: Sanity itself says an item that near "is not worth a trip and the check is not the point" (SN D.7). It is a constraint on VS-A junk placement: any junk a SCAN is meant to disprove must sit at least 3120 ticks of `T_home` from the console on the chosen reading, and DA's bounds must not be tuned to satisfy this. The cheap rung is the breath-step (pick up and feel `cue_load`, `C_check` is the pickup), valid only while no stowed class masks the carried one (`held_mass_cue`, `STOWED_CUE_FLOOR_CLASS` [PLACEHOLDER]).

### D.8 `sortie_dwell_envelope`

The sortie_dwell_envelope formula is defined as:

`runs_aboard = floor(t_aboard_s * TICK_RATE / SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR)` (Sanity's run is 12 ticks, 5 per second) ; `dwell_msu = runs_aboard * (ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE)` while standing quietly ; `t_stage1_s = ceil((SAN_MAX - STAGE_ENTER[1]) / (ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE)) / 5` ; the envelopes nest iff `t_target_s <= t_stage1_s` and the cell and reserve are sufficient *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the rate is `net_aboard = RING_EXPOSURE[ring] + ABOARD_EXPOSURE - REC_IDLE` (the ring term is 0 at ring 1, so the ring-1 figures below are unchanged); `t_stage1_s` is defined only for `net_aboard > 0` and is the sentinel `UNBOUNDED` otherwise (Sanity's registry rule (e) keeps `net_aboard` positive and under `DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN`, so the sentinel is a guard and not a reachable case); the second-sortie row is `L_start(2) = L_end(1) + settled_runs * (REC_SETTLED - RING_EXPOSURE[ring])` and `t_stage1_s(2) = ceil((L_start(2) - STAGE_ENTER[1]) / net_aboard) / 5`, with `settled_runs` `[UNV: Sanity, whether the pod leg and the home body count as settled]`)*
**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_aboard_s` | int s | 0 to about 480 | the session; counted from `pod_settle` to departure, the pod leg is not aboard (SN-5 E1) | time off the home body |
| `ABOARD_EXPOSURE` | int mSU per run | 20 (8 to 20) [PLACEHOLDER] | Sanity SN E1 | the dwell exposure |
| `REC_IDLE` | int mSU per run | 1 | Sanity (registry: `REC_IDLE`) | quiet recovery aboard |
| `SAN_MAX`, `STAGE_ENTER[1]` | int mSU | 100,000, 70,000 [PLACEHOLDER] | Sanity (registry: `SAN_MAX`, `STAGE_ENTER`) | the level scale and the stage-1 entry threshold |
| `SANITY_DRIFT_DIVISOR` | int ticks | 12 | Sanity | one drift run |
| `t_target_s` | int s | 300 (design target, not registered; 180 to 300 [PROVISIONAL: creative-director], Tuning Knobs) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | BS-20 | the upper bound of the median first-sortie *W_ABOARD* (arrival to the departing console press, D.1); the concept's five-minute loop is the W_LOOP figure |
| `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` | int ticks | 28,800 (8 min) [PLACEHOLDER] | Web Map D.5 | the fuel-floor safe sortie |
| `CELL_S` | int s | 1200 (600 to 2400) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | the lit-beam cell |

**Output Range:** `dwell_msu` 0 to 45,600 for 8 min (2400 runs at 19); the ordering check is boolean.

**Example:** `t_stage1_s = ceil(30000 / 19) / 5` = 1579 / 5 = **315.8 s (5.3 min)**, as Sanity states (SN:344, a playtest hypothesis). A sortie of 300 s W_ABOARD spends `1500 * 19` = 28,500 mSU, **95 percent** of the way to stage 1; an 8-minute one enters stage 1 at 5.3 min and spends 2.7 minutes in it. The orderings: design target 300 s <= stage-1 315.8 s (nests, with 15.8 s of slack) and stage-1 315.8 s < Web Map's safe sortie 480 s < `CELL_S` 1200 s. The fuel floor therefore tolerates a longer sortie than the sanity budget does; that is acceptable (the world shows the sanity cost, not a timer) and is recorded as C-BS-12 for Web Map and Sanity. The autosave loss ceiling (330 s, D.9) is a different axis. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* **Second sortie.** The nest above holds for a first sortie from `SAN_MAX`. After a 300 s W_ABOARD the level is 100,000 - 28,500 = 71,500 mSU, 1,500 above stage 1; a player who goes straight back out reaches stage 1 after `ceil(1500 / 19) / 5` = 79 / 5 = **15.8 s**. If the pod leg and the home walk and carry (about 1,431 ticks, 119 runs) count as settled, `119 * 12` = 1,428 mSU come back, `L_start(2)` is 72,928 and stage 1 arrives after `ceil(2928 / 19) / 5` = 155 / 5 = **31.0 s**. Restoring the full first-sortie envelope takes `28,500 / 12` = 2,375 settled runs = 28,500 ticks = **475 s (7.9 min)** at home. So the envelopes nest for a first sortie and for a sortie after about 8 minutes at home, and not for a second sortie in a row; this is a pacing finding for Sanity and Web Map (C-BS-12), not a Boarding rule, and it is routed, not repaired. *Not hashed.*

### D.9 `loss_window_after_cut`

The loss_window_after_cut formula is defined as:

`t_eligible = max(t_cut_done, t_last_gen + AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS)` ; `t_save = first AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS poll at or after t_eligible with the SL-5 gate open, never later than t_eligible + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` ; `lost_cut_window = t_save - t_cut_done` ; baseline without the read: `lost_cut_window_interval = t_next_interval - t_cut_done (+ defer)` ; death side (SL, cited): `loss_max = AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS + DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `t_cut_done` | tick | the tick of the V8 completion | Interaction (`last_salvage_T()`, ASK-BS-I4 answered) | when the cut finished |
| `t_last_gen` | tick | the last generation's `T` | Save/Load | when the world was last saved |
| `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | registry (Save/Load) | the minimum gap between interval or event generations |
| `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS` | int ticks | 10,800 | registry | the interval trigger |
| `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` | int ticks | 7200 | registry | the longest a soft gate defers before a `RISKY` write |
| `AUTOSAVE_RETRY_TICKS` | int ticks | 300 | Save/Load SL-5 | the gate re-evaluation period |
| `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` | int ticks | 1800 | registry | the downed time before death |
| gate states | set | hard: DOWNED, DEAD. Soft: row (a) endogenous Graph tell (EXOGENOUS cut descriptors open none); row (b) Attention tell with fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S * TICK_RATE` = 600 ticks left (FAIR mode, provisional); `GATE_SOFT_STATES` CHANNELLING, STAGGERED, IN_POD | Save/Load SL-5 | what defers a due save; `CARRY_WALK` is not a gate state |

**Output Range:** with the read, `lost_cut_window` is 0 to `AUTOSAVE_MIN_GAP_TICKS` plus a gate deferral of at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` (0 to 9000 ticks, 1800 + 7200); without it, up to `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_TICKS + AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` = 18,000 ticks (5 min) before the next interval generation, inside SL's death-side ceiling `loss_max` = **19,800 ticks (5.5 min)**.

**Example:** the last generation was written at tick 7600 and the cut completes at 10,000: age 2400 is over the 1800 gap, so the cut is eligible at once; the player is IDLE or `CARRY_WALK`, neither a gate state, so it is written at the next 300-tick poll (window at most 300 ticks, 5 s) and a death on the carry restores to *after the cut*. A cut that completes 600 ticks after a generation waits 1200 ticks (20 s) for the gap; if the player is capping (CHANNELLING) or a tell has under 600 ticks left, it defers further. A save deferred 7200 ticks is written `RISKY`, and a death within `DEATH_LOOP_WINDOW_TICKS` 3600 of restoring a `RISKY` generation steps back one generation (SL-8), so a doomed save cannot trap the player. Without the read, a cut 10 s after a generation waits for the interval generation 10,200 ticks (170 s) later, plus any gate deferral (at most `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` 7200 ticks, 120 s; 18,000 ticks, 300 s, in total at the worst). A whole 5-minute sortie therefore fits inside one worst-case loss. *Design-time.*

### D.10 `lamp_cell_budget`

The lamp_cell_budget formula is defined as:

`cell_share_pm = min(1000, floor(1000 * lit_ticks / (CELL_S * TICK_RATE)))` ; `sorties_per_cell = floor(CELL_S * TICK_RATE / lit_ticks)` for `lit_ticks >= 1`, and the sentinel `UNBOUNDED` for `lit_ticks = 0` (a dark sortie spends no cell, so the quotient does not exist) ; `sorties_to_strand = ceil(CELL_S * TICK_RATE / lit_ticks)` at a constant lit cost per sortie and no refill (the sortie in which the cell reaches 0) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the `lit_ticks = 0` guard, the clamp, the corrected ranges and `sorties_to_strand` are new; the first two expressions are otherwise unchanged)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `lit_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to `T_SORTIE_SAFE_TICKS` 28,800 for a safe sortie (a lit sortie may run longer; the clamp covers it); realistic play 1,278 (the reference line) to 18,000 (a 300 s survey) | the player's beam use (`toggle_beam`, SE-8 to SE-10) | ticks with the chest lamp on while aboard |
| `CELL_S` | int s | 1200 (600 to 2400) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | seconds of lit beam in a full cell; 72,000 ticks at the default |
| `RESERVE_S` | int s | 90 (45 to 240) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | engaged reserve breath, spent only in an unbreathable stretch (D.4) |
| `GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` | int | 2 (0 to 4) [PLACEHOLDER] | Suit | sticks in the start kit; each lights 150 s (`GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` [PLACEHOLDER]) |

**Output Range:** `cell_share_pm` 0 to 1000 (clamped), and 17 to 400 over the realistic and safe-sortie `lit_ticks` above. `sorties_per_cell` is `UNBOUNDED` at `lit_ticks` 0, **56 at the reference line** (28 to 112 across `CELL_S` 600 to 2400), 4 at the 300 s survey and 2 at the 28,800-tick safe-sortie bound. The earlier range "1 to about 56" and the earlier `lit_ticks` bound of 18,000 were wrong at both ends.

**Example:** the reference line from arrival to the console with the beam on throughout: `312 + 60 + 240 + 12 + 624 + 30` = 1278 ticks (the 12 is the pickup dwell and the 30 the console approach, both fixture dwells of the D.2 golden schedule) = 21.3 s: `floor(1000 * 1278 / 72000)` = **17 permille** of `CELL_S`, `floor(72000 / 1278)` = 56 sorties. A survey sortie lit for 300 s (18,000 ticks) is **250 permille**: `sorties_per_cell` 4 and `sorties_to_strand` 4. Each stick thrown aboard adds 180 AU raw (`U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER]) to D.2's intake. A 30-minute session of two derelict sorties in the survey style spends about half the cell, and there is no MVP refill until V7 `top_up` is promoted (IA OQ-4, Graph Open Question 20) or another cell policy is ruled (OQ-BS-4). **Campaign reading *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*:** a lit-beam player who surveys at 300 s per sortie has no cell from the fourth sortie, the authored sticks of the A route (2 + 2 + 4 = 8, each 150 s, B's shelves not counted) are then the only light, and when they are spent every derelict dock console is dark. That is the expected end of a long lit session and not a corner case, so the dark-dock strand of BS-19 is a campaign-length event and its acceptance (AC-BS-92) is run on the campaign-length cell. Lighting less (a deliberate dark walk with `toggle_beam`) saves cell and costs sight of the frost sill. *Design-time; the home ship's lamps are fixtures, not the chest lamp.*

## Edge Cases

*Format.* Each case is `If [condition]: [exact outcome]. [rationale]`, ends in its owner's rule-id (Boarding adds no behaviour, BS-1, so an outcome is always an owner's), and carries its id `EC-BS-n` at the end of the bold condition. Cases are grouped by theme; the audit additions of the checkpoint (K15: a DESTROYED node, a store module, a restore in `IN_POD`, the stowed plate, the door that mixes cold) are EC-BS-3, EC-BS-4, EC-BS-40, EC-BS-45 and EC-BS-20, and the dead-bus case (K4) is EC-BS-17. Numbers are design-time and rest on D.1 to D.10. Web Map's constants are registered [PLACEHOLDER] values (see the Format note of Core Rules).

### A. Zero, maximum and out of range

- **If the player presses `begin_cut` with the welder in hand (`START_EQUIPPED_SLOT` 2) or with no cutter in hand (EC-BS-1)**: the verb answers `NO_TOOL` as an outcome after the press, nothing is cut and no tool-channel tick accrues; `equip_tool` to slot 1 is the fix and its duration is unregistered, so every sortie time stays a lower bound (BS-2 step 1; IA-5, SE-11, SN OQ-SN-6). The wrong tool is an honest, cheap failure, not a hidden gate.
- **If the player presses `begin_cap` while carrying, or tries to `equip_tool` the welder with hands full (EC-BS-2)**: both are `BUSY`; the cap is a bare-handed welder act, so the order is cut, cap, then pick up, which follows from "never auto-carried" and not from a rule (BS-8, BS-9; IA-28, SE-11). A second `begin_cap` on an already capped stub is `INVALID_TARGET` (IA-26).
- **If the node being cut is DESTROYED (EC-BS-3)**: the cut yields `scrap_count` of its repair part and **no module**; there is nothing to carry, the parts are STOWED stock (they ride the body, not the hands), and the cut's noise is spent regardless. A DESTROYED machine is the loudest thing in its room and was never worth a module (IDB-17, LSG:772; K15).
- **If the module being cut is a store (the bus battery `b_a2`, the lamp battery `b_a1`) (EC-BS-4)**: its `stock` is discarded into the Graph's `discarded_*` counters so the conservation invariant still closes, and a reinstalled store starts at stock 0 (IDB-18 (i)). A battery has no fluid port, so it leaves no stub; the island re-solves on the next run and what ran on it goes dark, `b_a1` being the stored energy behind A's one residual lamp (DA-27, `[derived]`), so cutting it ends the visible unknown and is a cost the player caused (IDB-18, K15).
- **If a second module is wanted while one is carried (EC-BS-5)**: `pickup` answers `BUSY`; the player drops the first in a safe room and shuttles, or leaves it for a second hop, and a second hop costs a second `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] (D.6). The spare `mod_breaker_junction` and `htr_a` cannot ride in the same hop (IA-28, SE-12).
- **If a stowed stack cannot take the pickup (a glowstick shelf of 2 with 3 already stowed, or a second `part_hull_plate`) (EC-BS-6)**: the pickup is rejected with Suit's immediate `STACK_FULL`, nothing is taken and nothing is deleted; the stack stays on the shelf and the player throws or uses one first. `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` [PLACEHOLDER] 4 and the plate's `stack_max` 1 are the structural bound (IA-28, SE-12, IDB-15).
- **If the player holds a tool in the HAND holder when a CARRIED-class yield is picked up (EC-BS-7)**: the pickup is admitted, because "needs hands free" means no carried instance and a held tool is a different holder (SE-12). Recorded as an ask to Interaction to confirm the wording (ASK-BS-I5); until then the sortie tests treat it as admitted (IA-28).

### B. The carry-admitted set, doors and drops

- **If the console row is refused and the player presses the pod console while carrying (EC-BS-8)**: the press is `BUSY` with no prompt; the module must be dropped first and stays aboard exactly as left, persisting on the dormant derelict, and the carry-home branch is lost. **This state is not shippable for the MVP (BS-9):** it removes the carry home, the loop the MVP exists to test, so the producer treats the refusal as a stop and not a degrade. With a BREACH running it would be the abandon cost, but the failure escalates to the producer as a design blocker and is not an accepted state (BS-9 fallback; C-BS-1; IA-5, WM-24).
- **If the carried def is not in the focused hardpoint's `compatible_defs` (EC-BS-9)**: there is no prompt at all, an immediate answer that reads only the carried def and the hardpoint's declaration and never a module-body field, so it is not an oracle (BS-18; MSA-26, IA-11).
- **If a FAILED-`hp` instance is installed (EC-BS-10)**: it installs as a FAILED module, `condition_bp` carrying through unchanged, and the home island treats it as dead; no verb refused the install and no reason named the condition, and the consequence is visible in the Graph's channel (a dark lantern, a dry trail, no output) (BS-18; IDB-17, IDB-18, LS-22).
- **If a CLOSED door lies on the carry route (the home `door_cargo_s`, or a door the player shut behind them) (EC-BS-11)**: the door face shows no prompt and `interact` is `BUSY`; the carrier does `drop`, steps to the door's face anchor, V4 `set_opening` (30 ticks, QUIET), steps back and takes a lit `pickup`, about 30 ticks and two TAPs. A door shut as a containment tool is the player's own cost on the way out; during a TELL or BREACH the legal alternative is to abandon the module (BS-10, BS-13; IA-5, IA-24, IA-28).
- **If every candidate cell for a `drop` is blocked (a threshold square, nine blocked cells) (EC-BS-12)**: `drop` answers immediate `OBSTRUCTED` and the module stays carried; the player steps back a tile and tries again. Door cells answer `NONE` from `floor_tile_at`, so a drop never lands on one, and A and B corridors are 3 wide, so the risk cells are door thresholds only (BS-10; IA-28, DA-7).
- **If a SYSTEM-origin close targets a door whose leaf overlaps a carrier's foot box (EC-BS-13)**: the attempt becomes `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and retries every tick, landing on the first clear tick; the carrier cannot reopen it. MVP content has no SYSTEM close on a carry route, and a content change that adds one traps a carrier (IA-5), which is why it is an ask (BS-10; IA-24, ASK-BS-I2).
- **If a module is dropped and its section is then welded or cut away before the pickup (EC-BS-14)**: the loose item's position is `(section_id, pos_px)` section-local, so it goes with its section; a section that is cut away takes it with the Graph's `discarded_*` bookkeeping, a loss that belongs to the cut's owner (BS-23; LY-22, LS-16).

### C. The cut, the stubs and the faucet

- **If the machine being cut is running (the home scrubber, `htr_a` on a woken bus) (EC-BS-15)**: the cut is legal with no special case; the island re-solves on the next run, its dependants go STARVE, and the hum of the thing being cut drops: true-state audio in the consequence channel (LSG:976) that is not in LSG:44's enumerated list, used as true-state corroboration and not the falsifier of record until the Graph rules. The player who cuts their own air has made a P1 choice the world shows at once (BS-7, BS-4; LSG:773, FT:207, C-BS-4).
- **If `scr_a` is cut and carried home (EC-BS-16)**: it is a legal cut with no MVP destination (no vacant scrubber hardpoint, no `part_machine_kit` placed, no `remove_module`), so it comes home to be set down as a loose item; this is the honest "time the loot was garbage" beat and not a hidden fail state, and the reference cut is `htr_a` (BS-17; MSA-28, IDB-14, C-BS-3, CON Key Dynamics).
- **If `scr_a` is read on A's dead bus (EC-BS-17)**: every lantern is dark, `scr_a`'s and `pump_a`'s alike, and the room's O2 and temperature cannot tell an unpowered healthy scrubber from a FAILED one; the closed-list witnesses falsify "running" and nothing more. A SCAN gives an honest reading (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER], scanner in hand), and waking the bus with the spare breaker lights `pump_a`'s lantern while `scr_a`'s stays dark, at the price of the pump-stall clock (90 s) and wear doubled by `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION`. The world told the player it was dead; SCAN or the woken bus tells them it was broken (BS-17; DA-24, DA-25, AC-DA-111, K4).
- **If a stub is never capped, across a restore (EC-BS-18)**: `stubs[]` persists, the hiss persists and nothing forces anything; the leak is a standing consequence-channel truth, bounded by dormancy on a departed derelict (BS-8; IA-26, LS-17, WM-24).
- **If two seats target the same node or the same stub (co-op hook) (EC-BS-19)**: the second `begin_cut` is `BUSY` (one job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`), and a second `begin_cap` on a capped stub is `INVALID_TARGET`; the first lit `pickup` of the yield wins and the other seat's is `BUSY` or finds the item gone (BS-7; IA-34, IA-26).
- **If the player opens `door_a_spine_machine` and leaves it open (EC-BS-20)**: the "one lethal room" statement is a build-state invariant, so opening the door lets conduction mix the cold into `a_spine` (authored `T0` -12000 mC) for as long as it stays open; the lint prints the open-door projection, the tell stays the frost fan over the sill, and shutting the door behind (a bare-hand V4) is the player's containment act, with its cost on the way out (BS-10; DA-28, DA D.18).
- **If a cut node is cut again (EC-BS-21)**: the slot is a SALVAGED tombstone and the verb answers `NOT_SALVAGEABLE`; salvage-once is the faucet's whole shape, and the remaining salvage in a room is read from the empty pedestal rect, the scorched kerf and the stubs, never from a count (BS-16; IDB-17, LSG:243, LY-20, ART 7.4).
- **If an abandoned module is revisited on a derelict that went dormant (EC-BS-22)**: the module is in `loose[]` where it was left and the derelict stands as left; if it was dropped in the dark it needs light to re-take, and a dormant room's temperature and gas stand still until the player returns (BS-13, BS-16; LS-17, WM-24, WM-25, WM-28, IA-28).

### D. Tells, breach and retreat

- **If a tell opens as the cut begins (EC-BS-23)**: the cut completes (240 ticks is well under the played draw), leaving `T - 240` ticks; at the shortest played draw (`TELL_DRAW_LO` 900 [PLACEHOLDER]) a 13-tile carry fits by 36 ticks and a 14-tile carry does not, and at the 600-tick fixture floor 7 tiles is the most; drop-and-run always fits. The cut is loud and forfeits the stillness abort while it runs (BS-7, BS-13; D.3, AT-8, AT-10).
- **If a tell opens during the carry and the route is longer than the window (EC-BS-24)**: the decision is the player's, keep walking to the console or set the module down and run at `V_RUN` 3 against `V_CARRY` 1, and both are legal; the module persists where it fell, and re-taking it needs light. A drop is free (exempt from the lit rule), so a false alarm costs time and not the haul (BS-13; IA-28, PC-8, AT-8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(the decision is the intended shape and is not yet live at the current numbers, because the reference carry fits every played tell and a freeze is free; OQ-BS-27)*
- **If a BREACH is running and the carrier reaches the console (EC-BS-25)**: the hop is always allowed, `carry_ref` travels, and the departure ends the BREACH when the last recorded occupant leaves a non-home locus body; the hop pushes no stimulus and does not interrupt a TELL. The hop is the escape verb with the module still in the arms (BS-12, BS-13; WM-26, AT-22).
- **If a horror is met during a carry (EC-BS-26)**: nothing kills it; tools repel and distract, and the answer is retreat, stillness, darkness or a drop-and-run. Horror places nothing in salvage and has no edge to author (HE:376), so no rule here changes with a horror's presence (BS-13, BS-24).
- **If the player cancels a cut and goes still to abort a tell (EC-BS-27)**: the tell aborts after 594 ticks from an entry at `theta` and 630 from the level ceiling, against a ledger that keeps the partial for `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 600 inclusive; the partial survives only an immediate resume at the lowest entry levels, so the cut restarts from zero. Restraint costs the partial and keeps the module in place (BS-7; D.3, IA-16, AT-10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(once the cut has completed there is no partial to lose, so freezing then costs only the dwell, about 0.94 percent of `SAN_MAX` (D.3); whether to price it is part of OQ-BS-27)*
- **If `pod_hop` applies with a channel open or a cut ledger standing (EC-BS-28)**: the open channel aborts `ABORT(BODY)` and the ledger entry is Interaction's; the leg and settle alone are 945 ticks, longer than `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] 600, so on return the cut restarts from zero `[UNV: Interaction to confirm the ledger expires on global ticks while the derelict is dormant]`. The module is still in place on the derelict (BS-12; IA-40, IA-16).
- **If the player hops out and back to shed Attention (EC-BS-29)**: the level is not reset (a hop does not interrupt a TELL and the departure ring is held), each outbound hop costs `HOP_FUEL_ML` [PLACEHOLDER] 400 mL, and a departure ends only a BREACH, not a tell or the level; a hop is a retreat and not a reset (BS-13; WM-26, AT-22, D.6).

### E. Light and the dark

- **If a module was dropped in the dark (EC-BS-30)**: it cannot be re-taken until something lights it; the player throws a stick before the pickup (throwing is `BUSY` while carrying, channelling, staggered or downed (IA-29), so after the drop it is allowed) or returns with the beam on. The stick lights at landing, never before (BS-5; IA-28, IA-29, IA-6); the throw pushes one `U_LOUD` [PLACEHOLDER] at landing, so re-lighting a dark drop is a loud act (IA-29, IA-33).
- **If both start sticks and the shelf are spent (`GLOWSTICK_START_COUNT` [PLACEHOLDER] 2, `a_dock` shelf 2) (EC-BS-31)**: a dropped module outside any light waits for the chest beam, cell permitting; the home airlock's four-stick supply is the resupply for the next sortie, and `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` 8 bounds how many live lights can stand at once (BS-5, BS-19; DA-26, EM, IA-29).
- **If the cell is exhausted mid-carry (EC-BS-32)**: the beam goes dark; carrying and `drop` need no light, but `pickup` and the console press do, so the module can still be carried to the dock and the player is then in the BS-19 position (BS-19; SE, IA-6).
- **If the cell is spent, no stick remains and the dock console is unlit (EC-BS-33)**: `pod_hop` answers `NOT_LIT`, the player cannot hop and cannot re-take a dropped module, and because `a_dock` is survivable (`T0` -6000 mC) no death and no restore ever fires, a true soft-lock. The invariant and its candidate mechanisms (OQ-BS-4) are BS-19; the mitigations that narrow it are the two-stick shelf, the home four-stick supply and drop-throw-retake (one LOUD report per throw, IA-29) (BS-19, C-BS-9; WM E-16, IA-6, DA-26). **This state must not be reachable in a shipped build:** the invariant is BLOCKING for implementation (BS-19, AC-BS-92), so this entry describes the unmitigated state that the mechanism ruled in OQ-BS-4 (M1, M2 or M3) must remove (AC-BS-51a for M1 or M3, AC-BS-51b for M2); the loose-glowstick form of the content gate (`DOCK_CONSOLE_DARK_NO_STICK` as worded) is withdrawn as unable to close it, and there is no accepted-risk path. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* It is reached by play and not by a corner case: about 4 survey sorties spend the cell and the authored sticks are finite (D.10), so a long lit session ends here unless the cell policy or the mechanism prevents it.
- **If the player turns the beam off by choice while carrying (EC-BS-34)**: `toggle_beam` is available as an actor verb; the carry spends no cell and the lamp's exposure term leaves the job, but the frost sill and the hazard are seen only in the beam, and the console needs light to press. A dark carry is a legal Pillar 3 trade, not a rule (BS-5, BS-9; PC-8, EM).

### F. Hazard rooms and death

- **If the actor is DOWNED in `a_machine` while carrying (EC-BS-35)**: the Controller clears `carry_ref`, Interaction converts the module to a loose item at the foot origin within one tick (its hook runs at divisor 1, IA-28), and `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` 1800 starts; in the solo MVP death restores from the newest autosave (SL-7, SL-8), so the module is back on its pedestal if the newest autosave predates the cut, or at the kerf (loose or in the arms) if it followed the cut (EC-BS-42, D.9); either way the player's knowledge of the room is the carry-over. In co-op the loose module persists where it fell (BS-13; PC-9, SE-13, SE-24, SL-7).
- **If the player tries to strip `a_machine` (EC-BS-36)**: eight cuts of 240 ticks plus the crossing exceed the room's CRITICAL clock (2226 against 2184 ticks at `T0`), so the room tolerates one purposeful cut and a shuttle and not a strip; the lethal cold is the authored cost of the richer room (BS-17, BS-20; D.4, DA D.10, DA-28).
- **If the bus is woken and then `htr_a` is cut (EC-BS-37)**: the cut is legal on a running machine; the room stops warming and relaxes back toward `T_AMB_MC`, the pump-stall clock (on `pump_a`) continues unaffected, and the heater that made lingering survivable is the thing in the player's arms. The player caused it and the Graph's channel shows it (BS-17; LSG:773, DA-28, DA:1163).
- **If a module is dropped in B's vacuum room and must be re-taken (EC-BS-38)**: the re-take is another traverse against `RESERVE_S` [PLACEHOLDER] 90 s, and the DA bound for a carried round trip (56 tiles, 497 permille of the reserve) covers the traverse once, so a drop-and-return roughly doubles the exposure; that is a decision the player makes, with the hiss and the draught as the witnesses (BS-20; DA-28, SE D.11, D.4).

### G. Save, restore and autosave

- **If a restore lands mid-carry (EC-BS-39)**: `carry_ref` and the Suit's carried instance are restored together and cross-checked (`SUIT_RESTORE_CARRY_MISMATCH` on a mismatch), the Interaction `post_restore` never touches `carry_ref`, and a channel in progress is dropped (`RESTORE` abort; the Controller maps `CHANNELLING` to `IDLE`). A cut that completed before the autosave stands; one that did not is as if never begun (PC-17, SE-13, IA-15, IA-28).
- **If a restore lands in `IN_POD` (EC-BS-40)**: the mid-transit state follows Web Map's restore rules (the persisted leg parameters), `carry_ref` is restored with the Suit's instance, and the module is still in the arms on arrival; a save in `IN_POD` happens only after `AUTOSAVE_MAX_DEFER_TICKS` as a `RISKY` write because `IN_POD` is a soft-gate state (BS-21, BS-12; SL-5, WM-30, PC-17; K15).
- **If an autosave lands mid-carry (EC-BS-41)**: it is legal, because `CARRY_WALK` is not a gate state; the restore point is the carry, with the cut done and the stubs hissing, and the loss window of a later death is the carry only (BS-21; D.9, SL-5).
- **If the player dies after an autosave that followed the cut, or before it (EC-BS-42)**: after it, the world restores with the kerf, the hissing stubs and the module in the arms or on the floor, and the loss is the carry; before it, the node is intact, nothing of the take remains, and the player keeps what they learned of the room. Failure costs ship and body, never knowledge (BS-21; SL-7, SL-8, D.9, P5).

### H. Hallucination and the field checks

- **If a FAILED module's sprite or gauge reads alive (class (b), the MVP lie) (EC-BS-43)**: the module-body channel lies and the consequence channel does not; its lantern stays dark on a live bus, because lamp brightness is a closed-list witness (LSG:44); the decision is made on the closed-list witnesses and, where they cannot discriminate (the dead bus), on SCAN. There is no condition bar to trust (BS-6, BS-15, BS-17; LSG:44, LS-22).
- **If a phantom is reached for (class (d), VS-A) (EC-BS-44)**: a phantom has no subject and answers like an unlit object, so reaching for one is not a free oracle, and the junk that is real but dressed as a prize betrays itself by the breath-step, a SCAN, the palette leak or the trip home. Deferred with its blockers (BS-14, BS-15; EM-21, IA-13, SN-21).
- **If the stowed `part_hull_plate` is held (EC-BS-45)**: the plate lies in `a_spine` on the reference route, and a player who stows it masks the breath cue for the rest of the sortie, since `cue_load = max(carried_class, stowed_class)` reads HEAVY for any carried module; a pickup is `BUSY` while carrying, so the plate is taken on the way in or on a later hop. At the MVP the breath-step is perception only and records nothing, so no false verdict is stored; "drop the plate first" has no performable verb (ASK-BS-S1) (BS-11, BS-14; SE-28, SE OQ-8, DA-26, C-BS-10).
- **If a hallucination of an entity (class (a)) or of a crewmate (class (c)) appears during a carry (EC-BS-46)**: it hides the body and never the trail, and the human channel (a crewmate's beam colour and shape, their voice) stays true, so a false alarm costs a drop and a re-take under light, not the haul. Deferred to VS and VS-B (BS-15; SN-20, HE).
- **If any hallucination is applied to the cut's noise, the stub's hiss, the load's breath, the carry's pace, the time or the air (EC-BS-47)**: none can be; each price channel is truthful by its owner's rule, and a defect that let one lie is a P4 violation (BS-15; IA-33, LSG:44, SE-28, PC-8).

### I. Content, budgets and loaded sets

- **If the post-weld sortie to A (S4) is made (EC-BS-48)**: `S_int` is 144 against `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120, over by 24 (DA OQ-DA-20, owned by DA and Interaction); Boarding seeds no loose item, raises no `P` above `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24, and a solo sortie holds at most one carried instance, so drops are one per module taken (BS-20; DA-32, IA D.10, C-BS-7).
- **If any sortie invariant is checked (EC-BS-49)**: it is checked over all five loaded sets S0 to S4 and both sortie orders, never only the first sortie to A: the route legs are per section and unaffected by what else is loaded, the subject budget is the one that moves (BS-20; DA-32, PF-5).
- **If the home pool is under the floor when a haul is installed or a hop is pressed (EC-BS-50)**: an outbound hop is refused `FUEL_LOW` but the module is installable at home; a return hop from a derelict is accepted at any pool, including 0, so no sortie strands a haul (BS-12; WM-20, WM D.4).
- **If the arrival cell at the destination is blocked (EC-BS-51)**: `pod_hop` answers `ARRIVAL_BLOCKED`, a defect of the dock's clear cell (`SPAWN_BLOCKED` lint); the module stays CARRIED, the player stays at the console and no cargo state changes (BS-2 step 2; WM-9, WM-15, DA-3).

### J. Co-op hooks (Vertical Slice B; solo first)

- **If a second and third seat board (EC-BS-52)**: each seat has its own `carry_ref` and its own stowed stacks, pod capacity is the seats (one at MVP, WM-5; the VS-B count is the concept's co-op range, owner Web Map at VS-B), and the haul is a division of labour; nothing in this GDD keys on a single seat (BS-12; PC-8, SE-12, WM-5).
- **If a crewmate is DOWNED while carrying (EC-BS-53)**: the Controller clears their `carry_ref` and the module falls at their foot origin; there is no stealing from a crewmate's hands and no reward for harm, and a revive is Interaction's and the Suit's (BS-24 G-8; PC-9, SE-13).
- **If a cutter is swung near a crewmate (EC-BS-54)**: a cut channel targets a SALVAGE_MODULE subject and never an ACTOR, so nothing is harmed by accident. Harm to a crewmate is not a choice a sane player can make: it needs a deliberate lit `begin_harm` channel (IA-31) **and is admitted only while the attacker's sanity is system-verified below the harm threshold** (Sanity's `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`, which reads the SIM `harm_eligible` bit; a sane actor's attempt is `NOT_ELIGIBLE` and does nothing; SN-11); **the victim sees the attacker's palette leak and sanity tell before eligibility opens** (`tell_on` leads `harm_eligible` by `SAN_TELL_LEAD_S`, SN-10); **and the incident is attributed to the web** (Interaction's call to Sanity's Class B mutator `record_harm_event(actor, victim, tick)` at harm-channel completion, SN-13), not to the player. Friendly fire is lethal by the user's decision ("death is a bitch"), but only the web's work can open it (Pillar 4(c); anti-pillar: no intentional PvP; BS-24 G-1 and G-8; IA-25, IA-31). Vertical Slice B: at MVP (one seat) no target exists.

### K. Degenerate strategies

- **If the player strips a wreck node by node (EC-BS-55)**: each module is a carried trip, a hop and a walk, the faucet is the authored node list and salvage-once, the left derelict persists as left, and a hazard room is lethal at eight cuts; there is no safe farm and no refill (BS-16; DA-21, IDB-17, IDB-29, D.4).
- **If the player cuts several modules in one visit and carries one per hop (EC-BS-56)**: the noise is Attention's whatever the carry (a chain of cuts trips a tell in the fifth cycle with the beam on, D.2), the yields lie where they fell and the derelict persists; the stockpile on the deck is a cost the web hears and not a store the player owns (BS-7; IA-33, D.2).
- **If the player stands still aboard to wait out a threat (EC-BS-57)**: listening is a stance and idle pushes nothing, but dwell is Sanity's price: 19 mSU per run net, stage 1 after 315.8 s; there is no refuge in waiting (BS-4; PC-10, SN E1, D.8).
- **If the player tries to cut in the dark to dodge exposure (EC-BS-58)**: a dark cut is impossible, the act gate needs the target lit, and a lit job is a visible job in the exposure ledger (BS-5; IA-6, EM).
- **If the player seals doors behind them to contain what follows (EC-BS-59)**: it is a legal Pillar 3 act with a cost on the way out (a closed door met while carrying, EC-BS-11); nothing in Boarding forbids it and no content closes a door for them (BS-10; IA-24).

### L. Simultaneous rules

- **If the cut completes on the run in which the level crosses `ATT_THRESHOLD` and the tell opens (EC-BS-60)**: both happen, because the cut's effect lands at completion only (IA-25) and the tell is drawn at entry from its own stream (AT-8) and neither owner reads the other; the player meets the tell with the yield on the deck and not in the arms, and the choice of D.3 (carry, or drop-free run) is available at once. The LOUD one-shot is part of the intake that opened the tell, which is the "weigh the noise" cost made visible (BS-7; IA-25, IA-33, AT-8).
- **If the player cancels a cut on the same tick the actor is staggered or an abort cause fires (EC-BS-61)**: `cancel_channel` wins and the hook finds no record, so the ledger entry is written once by the cancel and the abort cue is not played (IA-15); the stagger itself keeps the module (BS-9; PC-8, PC-9).
- **If a tell's abort condition and its breach-due tick fall on the same run (EC-BS-62)**: the abort wins, one transition, and no breach opens; a player who is below `theta_HOLD` for `ABORT_HOLD_TICKS` [PLACEHOLDER] at that run is saved by the margin they earned (BS-13; AT-10).

## Dependencies

Rows list only what the consumer must supply or read at the boundary (the quoted items of section "Interactions with Other Systems"), not a guess at a neighbour's internal state. **Direction** is from this GDD's side. **Strength** is the index's for the five direct rows (Dependency Map position 11) and this GDD's judgement for the indirect reads. **Status** is the `> **Status**` header of each file, re-read on 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(at authoring, 2026-10-26, Perception Filter and Adaptive Audio & Tells were In Design)*: *Designed* means locked and cited by rule-id; *In Design* means the contract is provisional. Boarding & Salvage is a leaf: no designed system depends on it, so there is no cycle.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
